&L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
        & -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\\
        = & \ 0.
\end{align*}
Additionally, $\epsilon_i$ propagates to $R_n$ indirectly through the remaining observations $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ and $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$ (see equation \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}). Let $\widehat U^\prime(S_j)$ be the value of $\widehat U(S_j)$ when we change $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$. Then, this cross-influence difference is bounded by
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
        & -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right|\\
        \leq & C\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_diff_f}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of rate $\log(n)$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in the empirical risk resulting from changing $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n) n^{-1}\|f - f^\prime\|_{\infty}$ and this uniformly holds for all $i$. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields the following $\psi_2$-norm concentration bound for the centered process:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\log(n) n^{-1/2}\|f - f^\prime\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Second Layer of Randomness: $\xi_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Fixing $X,U$, we view the conditional expectation of the empirical risk as a function of $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i,\ldots, \xi_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i,\ldots, \xi_n)$ and $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i^\prime,\ldots, \xi_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, changing $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$ only affects the observed treatment $A_i$. Let $A_i^\prime$ be the value of $A_i$ when we change $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$. Then, the perturbation in the empirical risk is bounded by

\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
        & -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right|\\
        \leq & C\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_diff_f}. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields the following $\psi_2$-norm concentration bound for the centered process:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C n^{-1/2}\|f - f^\prime\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Third Layer of Randomness: $U(S_i)$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
The random variable $U$ is a Gaussian process with covariance matrix $\Sigma_n$. Let $\widetilde U=\Sigma^{-1/2} U$ where $\widetilde U$ is a vector of i.i.d. standard normal distribution. We then view the empirical risk as a function of $\widetilde U$ whose derivative can be calculated as follows:
\begin{align*}
    &\partial_{ \widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \frac{\partial U}{ \partial \widetilde  U}\cdot  \E\left[\partial_{ U }\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \Sigma_n^{1/2}\E\left[\partial_{U(S_1)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right\},\ldots,\partial_{U(S_n)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]
\end{align*}
Furthermore, for every $i$, the derivative of the empirical risk difference with respect to $U(S_i)$ decomposes as:
\begin{align*}
    &\E\left[\partial_{U(S_i)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \sum_{j\neq i}^n  \alpha_{i,j}\underbrace{\E\left[\left\{\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_f}}}\\
    &+ \underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)   - \partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\bigg| U,X\right]}_{=\;0}\\
    \leq &\;Cn^{-1}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
The $\partial_{Y_i}$ term vanishes because $Y_i$ does not appear in $L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)$ (see the preamble of this section). The bound on the $\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}$ term follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_f}, and the $\log(n)$ factor accounts for the $O(\log n)$ nonzero $\alpha_{i,j}$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}). Then, the Lipschitz constant of the conditional expectation of the empirical risk difference with respect to $\widetilde U$ given $U,X$ has the following bound:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\partial_{ \widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\bigg| U,X\right]\right\|_2\\
    \leq & C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
By Theorem \ref{thm:gaussian_concentration}, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}



\noindent\textbf{Fourth Layer of Randomness: $X_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$

We treat the empirical risk as a function of the covariate vector $(X_1, \ldots,X_i,\ldots, X_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(X_1, \ldots,X_i,\ldots, X_n)$ and $(X_1, \ldots,X_i^\prime,\ldots, X_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ affects the $f,f^\prime$-difference of the loss $L_i^{(\delta_n)}$ directly through the covariate $X_i$ in $f(\cdot,X_i)$ and $p(\cdot|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$, and also affects $L_j^{(\delta_n)}$ indirectly through the estimated latent structures $\{\widehat U(S_j)\}_{j\neq i}$ where $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$.

Specifically, let $O_i:=(Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i)$ and let $O_i^\prime$ be the observation when we change $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$. Then, the impact of changing $X_i$ on the $f,f^\prime$-difference of the loss yields:
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i;\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
        & -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i^\prime;\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right|\\
        \leq & C\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_f}. Additionally, $X_i$ propagates to the empirical risk indirectly through the remaining observations $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ and $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$ (see equation \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}). Let $O_j^\prime$ be the $j$-th observation when we change $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$. Then, this cross-influence difference is similarly bounded by
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j;\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
        & -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j^\prime;\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right|\\
        \leq & C\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty
\end{align*}
The last inequality again follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_f}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of rate $\log(n)$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in the empirical risk resulting from changing $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n)n^{-1}\|f-f^\prime\|_{\infty}$ and this uniformly holds for all $i$. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields the following $\psi_2$-norm concentration bound for the centered process:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}

\noindent \textbf{Sub-Gaussianity:} Combining the results above, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & \underbrace{\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\epsilon$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\xi$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $U$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $X$}}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty + C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty\\
    \leq & C\left(1+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\right)\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}


\subsubsection{Sub-Gaussianity over $p$}

In this section, for any $p,p^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_{p}$, we provide the upper bound for
$$\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Specifically, for fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, $\psi$, and $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$,
\begin{align*}
    &R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\\
    := & \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n \left(L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right)
    \\
    = & \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n \left(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat{U}(S_i)\right)\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Note that $Y_i$ does \emph{not} cancel in this difference; the residual $(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat{U}(S_i))$ is multiplied by the difference of reciprocal propensities.
We use the conditional expectation to decompose the $\psi_2$-norm above into four terms, i.e., the randomness from $(X_i,U(S_i),\xi_i,\epsilon_i)$ as in \eqref{ref:randomness_decomposition} and provide the upper bound of each term.

\noindent\textbf{First Layer Randomness: $\epsilon$}

\noindent We first provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
We treat the empirical risk as a function of the outcome error vector $(\epsilon_1, \ldots,\epsilon_i,\ldots, \epsilon_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(\epsilon_1, \ldots,\epsilon_i,\ldots, \epsilon_n)$ and $(\epsilon_1, \ldots,\epsilon_i^\prime,\ldots, \epsilon_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ affects the empirical risk through the observed outcome $Y_i$ and the estimated latent structures $\{\widehat U(S_j)\}_{j\neq i}$ where $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$.

Since $Y_i$ appears in $L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)$ through the residual factor, changing $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ (which changes $Y_i$ to $Y_i^\prime := Y_i - \epsilon_i + \epsilon_i^\prime$) has a direct nonzero effect on the deviation process. Bounding this direct channel,
\begin{align*}
&\left|\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right.\\
        &\quad\left. -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right|\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right|
        \leq  C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}. Additionally, $\epsilon_i$ propagates to $R_n$ indirectly through the remaining observations $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ and $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$ (see equation \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}). Let $\widehat U^\prime(S_j)$ be the value of $\widehat U(S_j)$ when we change $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$. Then, this cross-influence difference is also bounded by
\begin{align*}
&\left|\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right.\\
        &\quad\left.-\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right|\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right|
        \leq  C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality again follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of rate $\log(n)$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in the empirical risk resulting from changing $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n) n^{-1}\|p - p^\prime\|_{\infty}$ and this uniformly holds for all $i$. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields the following $\psi_2$-norm concentration bound for the centered process:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\log(n) n^{-1/2}\|p - p^\prime\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Second Layer of Randomness: $\xi_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Fixing $X,U$, we view the conditional expectation of the empirical risk as a function of $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i,\ldots, \xi_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i,\ldots, \xi_n)$ and $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i^\prime,\ldots, \xi_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, changing $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$ only affects the observed treatment $A_i$. Let $A_i^\prime$ be the value of $A_i$ when we change $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$. Then, the perturbation in the empirical risk is bounded by
\begin{align*}
&\left|\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right.\\
        &\quad\left.-\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right|\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right|
        \leq  C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C n^{-1/2}\|p - p^\prime\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Third Layer of Randomness: $U(S_i)$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
The random variable $U$ is a Gaussian process with covariance matrix $\Sigma_n$. Let $\widetilde U=\Sigma^{-1/2} U$ where $\widetilde U$ is a vector of i.i.d. standard normal distribution. We then view the empirical risk as a function of $\widetilde U$ whose derivative can be calculated as follows:
\begin{align*}
    &\partial_{ \widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \Sigma_n^{1/2}\E\left[\partial_{U(S_1)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right\},\ldots,\partial_{U(S_n)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right].
\end{align*}
Furthermore, for every $i$, the derivative of the empirical risk difference with respect to $U(S_i)$ decomposes as:
\begin{align*}
    &\E\left[\partial_{U(S_i)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \sum_{j\neq i}^n  \alpha_{i,j}\underbrace{\E\left[\left\{\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_p}}}\\
    &+ \underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)   - \partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\bigg| U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y_diff_p}}}\\
    \leq &\;Cn^{-1}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty + Cn^{-1}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty\\
    \leq &\;Cn^{-1}\log(n)\cdot\left(\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}+1\right)\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
The bound on the $\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}$ term follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_p}, and the $\log(n)$ factor accounts for the $O(\log n)$ nonzero $\alpha_{i,j}$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}). The $\partial_{Y_i}$ term is bounded by Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y_diff_p} and contributes an $O(n^{-1})$ factor. Then, the Lipschitz constant of the conditional expectation of the empirical risk difference with respect to $\widetilde U$ given $U,X$ has the following bound:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\partial_{ \widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\bigg| U,X\right]\right\|_2\\
    \leq & C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\left(\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}+1\right)\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
By Theorem \ref{thm:gaussian_concentration}, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\left(\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}+1\right)\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Fourth Layer of Randomness: $X_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
We treat the empirical risk as a function of the covariate vector $(X_1, \ldots,X_i,\ldots, X_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(X_1, \ldots,X_i,\ldots, X_n)$ and $(X_1, \ldots,X_i^\prime,\ldots, X_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ affects the $p,p^\prime$-difference of the loss $L_i^{(\delta_n)}$ directly through the covariate $X_i$ in $f(\cdot,X_i)$, $p(\cdot|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$, and $p^\prime(\cdot|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$, and also affects $L_j^{(\delta_n)}$ indirectly through the estimated latent structures $\{\widehat U(S_j)\}_{j\neq i}$ where $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$.

Specifically, let $O_i:=(Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i)$ and let $O_i^\prime$ be the observation when we change $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$. Then, the direct impact of changing $X_i$ on the $p,p^\prime$-difference of the loss yields:
\begin{align*}
&\left|L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i;\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right.\\
        &\quad\left. -\left[L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right|\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right|
        \leq  C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}. Additionally, $X_i$ propagates to the empirical risk indirectly through the remaining observations $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ and $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$ (see equation \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}). Let $O_j^\prime$ be the $j$-th observation when we change $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$. Then, this cross-influence difference is similarly bounded by
\begin{align*}
&\left|L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j;\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right.\\
        &\quad\left.-\left[L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_j^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right|\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right|
        \leq  C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality again follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of rate $\log(n)$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in the empirical risk resulting from changing $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n)n^{-1}\|p-p^\prime\|_{\infty}$ and this uniformly holds for all $i$. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}

\noindent \textbf{Sub-Gaussianity:} Combining the results above, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & \underbrace{\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)-\E\left[\cdots|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\epsilon$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[\cdots|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\xi$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X,U\right]-\E\left[\cdots|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $U$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X\right]-\E\left[\cdots\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $X$}}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty + C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\left(\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}+1\right)\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty\\
    \leq & C\left(1+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\right)\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}


\subsubsection{Sub-Gaussianity over $\psi$}

In this section, for any $\psi,\psi^\prime$, we provide the upper bound for
$$\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Specifically, 
\begin{align*}
    &R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\\
    := & \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n \left(L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right),
\end{align*}
where, by contrast with the $f$- and $p$-perturbation cases, the parameter $\psi$ enters the $i$-th term through the estimated latent confounder $\widehat{U}(S_i,\psi)=\sum_{j\neq i}\alpha_{ij}(\psi)\widehat{R}_j$: both the data fed into the loss and the implicit structure of the loss itself change when $\psi$ varies. Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi} already accounts for both effects and yields
\begin{align}
    \sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|
    \leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\sqrt{\log(n)}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.\label{eq:Lipschitz_psi}
\end{align}
For brevity, we write $B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}:= C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\sqrt{\log(n)}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ for the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:Lipschitz_psi}.
We use the conditional expectation to decompose the $\psi_2$-norm into four terms as in \eqref{ref:randomness_decomposition} and provide the upper bound of each.

% \textcolor{red}{To bound this one, we should not use the same technique as the previous case where we investigate the change in $L_i$ first, then aggregate them to calculate the change in empirical risk $R_n$. This is because the upper bound in Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi} is too loose. If we use $\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|$ as the norm, to upper bound the change in matrix/vector is much tighter than upper bounding the change in a single entry.}

\noindent\textbf{First Layer Randomness: $\epsilon$}

\noindent We first provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
We treat the empirical risk as a function of the outcome error vector $(\epsilon_1, \ldots,\epsilon_i,\ldots, \epsilon_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(\epsilon_1, \ldots,\epsilon_i,\ldots, \epsilon_n)$ and $(\epsilon_1, \ldots,\epsilon_i^\prime,\ldots, \epsilon_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ affects the empirical risk through the observed outcome $Y_i$ and the estimated latent structures $\{\widehat U(S_j)\}_{j\neq i}$ where $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$.

The direct channel on the $i$-th term: since $\widehat{U}(S_i,\psi)=\sum_{j\neq i}\alpha_{ij}(\psi)\widehat{R}_j$ does \emph{not} depend on $\epsilon_i$ (the sum excludes $j=i$), a change $\epsilon_i\to\epsilon_i^\prime$ only alters $Y_i\to Y_i^\prime:=Y_i-\epsilon_i+\epsilon_i^\prime$ in the $i$-th loss difference. This direct effect is bounded by
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p) \\
    & - \left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\\
    \leq & 2 \sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|\\
    \leq & 2B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}.
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}. Additionally, $\epsilon_i$ propagates to $R_n$ indirectly through $\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi)$ and $\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi^\prime)$ for $j\neq i$ with $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$, by altering $\widehat{R}_i = Y_i - f(A_i,X_i)$. Let $\widehat{U}^\prime(S_j,\psi)$ (resp.\ $\widehat{U}^\prime(S_j,\psi^\prime)$) denote these estimates after the change. The resulting perturbation on the $j$-th loss difference is bounded by
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j,\psi),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j,\psi^\prime),X_j);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p) \\
    & - \left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j,\psi),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j,\psi^\prime),X_j);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\\
    \leq & 2 \sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|\\
    \leq & B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}.
\end{align*}
% \begin{align*}
%     &\sum_{j\neq i} 1(\alpha_{i,j}\neq 0)\left(L^{(\delta_n)}_j(\theta;\psi,f,p)\big|_{\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi)} - L^{(\delta_n)}_j(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\big|_{\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi^\prime)}\right)\\
%     & \quad +\sum_{j\neq i} 1(\alpha_{i,j}\neq 0)\left(L^{(\delta_n)}_j(\theta;\psi,f,p)\big|_{\widehat{U}^\prime(S_j,\psi)} - L^{(\delta_n)}_j(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\big|_{\widehat{U}^\prime(S_j,\psi^\prime)}\right)\\
%     \leq & \sum_{j\neq i} 1(\alpha_{i,j}\neq 0) \sup\left|\frac{\partial L_j^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)}{\partial \widehat U(S_j)}\right|\left(\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi)-\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi^\prime)+\widehat{U}^\prime(S_j,\psi)-\widehat{U}^\prime(S_j,\psi^\prime)\right)\\
%     \leq & \gamma_n^{-1/2}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\left\{\sum_{j\neq i} 1(\alpha_{i,j}\neq 0)\right\}^{1/2}\left(\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi)-\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi^\prime)\|_2 + \|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^\prime(\psi)-\widehat{\mathbf{U}^\prime}(\psi^\prime)\|_2\right)\\
%     \leq &C\sqrt{\log(n)n}\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
% \end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:widehat_U_diff_psi_L2}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of rate $\log(n)$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in the empirical risk resulting from changing $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n) n^{-1} B_\psi$ and this uniformly holds for all $i$. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)\, n^{-1/2} B_\psi
    = C\log(n)^{3/2}\,n^{-1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Second Layer of Randomness: $\xi_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Fixing $X,U$, we view the conditional expectation of the empirical risk as a function of $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i,\ldots, \xi_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i,\ldots, \xi_n)$ and $(\xi_1, \ldots,\xi_i^\prime,\ldots, \xi_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, changing $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$ only affects the observed treatment $A_i$; in particular, it does \emph{not} affect $\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi)$ or $\widehat{U}(S_j,\psi^\prime)$ for any $j$. Let $A_i^\prime$ be the value of $A_i$ when we change $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$. Then, the perturbation in the empirical risk is bounded by
\begin{align*}
&\left|\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i,\psi),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right.\\
        &\quad\left.-\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i,\psi),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right|\\
        \leq  & 2\sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|
        \leq  2B_\psi,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}. There is no cross-influence from $\xi_i$ to other terms. Consequently, the bounded-difference constant is $Cn^{-1}B_\psi$ for each $i$, and applying McDiarmid's inequality yields:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\, n^{-1/2} B_\psi
    = C\log(n)^{1/2}\,n^{-1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Third Layer of Randomness: $U(S_i)$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
The random variable $U$ is a Gaussian process with covariance matrix $\Sigma_n$. Let $\widetilde U=\Sigma^{-1/2} U$ where $\widetilde U$ is a vector of i.i.d. standard normal distribution. We then view the empirical risk as a function of $\widetilde U$ whose derivative can be calculated as follows:
\begin{align*}
    &\partial_{ \widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \Sigma_n^{1/2}\E\left[\partial_{U(S_1)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right\},\ldots,\partial_{U(S_n)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right].
\end{align*}
Furthermore, for every $i$, the derivative of the empirical risk difference with respect to $U(S_i)$ decomposes as:
\begin{align*}
    &\E\left[\partial_{U(S_i)}\left\{R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right\}\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    = & \sum_{j\neq i}^n  \left\{\alpha_{i,j}(\psi)\E\left[\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\bigg| U,X\right]-\alpha_{i,j}(\psi^\prime)\E\left[\partial_{\widehat U^\prime(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\bigg| U,X\right]\right\}\\
    &+ \E\left[\partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)   - \partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\bigg| U,X\right]\\
    \leq & \sum_{j\neq i}^n  \left\{\alpha_{i,j}(\psi)\underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p) - \partial_{\widehat U^\prime(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\bigg| U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_psi}}}\right\}\\
    & + \sum_{j\neq i}^n  \left\{\underbrace{\left(\alpha_{i,j}(\psi)-\alpha_{i,j}(\psi^\prime)\right)}_{\text{bounded by $\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi)^\prime\|_{\text{op}}$}}\underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{\widehat U^\prime(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\bigg| U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by $C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$}}\right\}\\
    & + \underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)   - \partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\bigg| U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y_diff_psi}}}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)\,(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}\log(n)^{1/2}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    & + C\log(n)^{1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda^{-1/2}\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    & + C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(n)}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)^{3/2}\,(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\end{align*}

Then, the Lipschitz constant of the conditional expectation of the empirical risk difference with respect to $\widetilde U$ given $U,X$ has the following bound:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\partial_{ \widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{(\delta)}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\bigg| U,X\right]\right\|_2\\
    \leq & C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\log(n)^{3/2}\,(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{align*}
By Theorem \ref{thm:gaussian_concentration}, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\log(n)^{3/2}\,(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Fourth Layer of Randomness: $X_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
We treat the empirical risk as a function of the covariate vector $(X_1, \ldots,X_i,\ldots, X_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(X_1, \ldots,X_i,\ldots, X_n)$ and $(X_1, \ldots,X_i^\prime,\ldots, X_n)$. According to Table \ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ affects the $p,p^\prime$-difference of the loss $L_i^{(\delta_n)}$ directly through the covariate $X_i$ in $f(\cdot,X_i)$, $p(\cdot|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$, and $p^\prime(\cdot|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$, and also affects $L_j^{(\delta_n)}$ indirectly through the estimated latent structures $\{\widehat U(S_j)\}_{j\neq i}$ where $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$. Specifically, let $(Y_i^\prime,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i^\prime)$ be the observation when we change $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$. Then, the direct impact of changing $X_i$ on the $\psi,\psi^\prime$-difference of the loss yields:

\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\\
    & - \left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i^\prime);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i^\prime,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i^\prime);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p) \right]\\
    \leq & 2 \sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|\\
    \leq & 2B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}.
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}. Additionally, $X_i$ propagates to the empirical risk indirectly through the remaining observations $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ and $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$ (see equation \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}). We write the estimated latent process at $j$-th observation as $\widehat U(S_j,X_i;\psi)$ as a function of $X_i$ and $\psi$. Then, this cross-influence difference is similarly bounded by
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j,X_i;\psi),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j,X_i;\psi^\prime),X_j);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\\
    & - \left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j,X_i^\prime;\psi),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j,X_i^\prime;\psi^\prime),X_j);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p) \right]\\
    \leq & 2 \sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|\\
    \leq & 2B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}.
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of rate $\log(n)$ (see equation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in the empirical risk resulting from changing $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ is bounded by $\log(n)B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}$. Consequently, applying McDiarmid's inequality yields:
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq &\log(n)n^{-1/2}B_{\psi,\psi^\prime}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)^{3/2}n^{-1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\end{align*}

\noindent \textbf{Sub-Gaussianity:} Combining the results above i.e., sub-Gaussianity from four layers of randomness, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
    \leq & \underbrace{\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)-\E\left[\cdots|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\epsilon$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[\cdots|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\xi$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X,U\right]-\E\left[\cdots|U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $U$}}\\
    & + \underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X\right]-\E\left[\cdots\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $X$}}\\
    \leq & C\log(n)^{3/2}\,n^{-1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    & + C\log(n)^{1/2}\,n^{-1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    & + C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\log(n)^{3/2}\,(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    & +C\log(n)^{3/2}n^{-1/2}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    \leq & C\left(\delta_n^{-1}+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}(1+\gamma_n^{-1})\right)\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\log(n)^{3/2}\,n^{-1/2}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Sub-Gaussianity: Absolute Bound for $R_n$}
\label{subsec:sub-gaussian-absolute}

In this section we provide the upper bound for the centered absolute empirical risk:
$$\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Unlike the difference-based bounds above, there is no cancellation: every term in $L_i^{(\delta_n)}$ is present and $Y_i$ appears directly in the IPW term. We decompose the $\psi_2$-norm into four layers of randomness as in \eqref{ref:randomness_decomposition} and bound each layer in turn.

\noindent\textbf{First Layer Randomness: $\epsilon$}

\noindent We first provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
We treat the empirical risk as a function of the outcome error vector $(\epsilon_1,\ldots,\epsilon_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate. According to Table~\ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ affects $R_n^{(\delta_n)}$ through (i) the observed outcome $Y_i\to Y_i^\prime:=Y_i-\epsilon_i+\epsilon_i^\prime$, and (ii) the estimated latent confounder $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ with $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$.

The direct effect on the $i$-th loss is bounded using Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_bound}:
\begin{align*}
\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i^\prime,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)\right|
\leq 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_i\right| \leq 2C.
\end{align*}
The indirect effect on each $j$-th loss (through $\widehat U^\prime(S_j)$, the value of $\widehat U(S_j)$ after the change) is similarly bounded:
\begin{align*}
\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_j,A_j,\widehat U^\prime(S_j),X_j);\theta,\psi,f,p)\right|
\leq 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_j\right| \leq 2C,
\end{align*}
again by Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_bound}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of order $\log(n)$ (see \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), the total perturbation in $R_n^{(\delta_n)}$ from changing $\epsilon_i$ to $\epsilon_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n)\,n^{-1}$, uniformly in $i$. Applying McDiarmid's inequality:
\begin{align*}
\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}
\leq C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Second Layer of Randomness: $\xi_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
Fixing $X,U$, we view the conditional expectation as a function of $(\xi_1,\ldots,\xi_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate. According to Table~\ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, changing $\xi_i$ to $\xi_i^\prime$ only affects $A_i$; in particular, $\widehat U(S_j)$ is unaffected for all $j$. Let $A_i^\prime$ be the treatment under $\xi_i^\prime$. The perturbation in $R_n^{(\delta_n)}$ is bounded using Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_bound}:
\begin{align*}
\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i^\prime,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)\right|
\leq 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_i\right| \leq 2C.
\end{align*}
Since $\xi_i$ has no cross-influence through $\widehat U$, the bounded-difference constant is $2C\,n^{-1}$ for each $i$. Applying McDiarmid's inequality:
\begin{align*}
\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}
\leq C\,n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Third Layer of Randomness: $U(S_i)$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
The random variable $U$ is a Gaussian process with covariance matrix $\Sigma_n$. Let $\widetilde U=\Sigma_n^{-1/2}U$ where $\widetilde U\sim N(0,I_n)$. We view $\E[R_n^{(\delta_n)}|X,U]$ as a function of $\widetilde U$ and compute:
\begin{align*}
\partial_{\widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\bigg|U,X\right]
= \Sigma_n^{1/2}\E\left[\partial_{U(S_1)}R_n^{(\delta_n)},\ldots,\partial_{U(S_n)}R_n^{(\delta_n)}\bigg|U,X\right].
\end{align*}
For every $i$, the derivative of $R_n^{(\delta_n)}$ with respect to $U(S_i)$ decomposes (via Table~\ref{tab:filled_jacobian}) as:
\begin{align*}
\E\left[\partial_{U(S_i)}R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\bigg|U,X\right]
=&\;\sum_{j\neq i}\alpha_{i,j}\underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{\widehat U(S_j)}R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\bigg|U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_U}}}\\
&+\underbrace{\E\left[\partial_{Y_i}R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\bigg|U,X\right]}_{\text{bounded by Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y}}}\\
\leq &\; Cn^{-1}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2} + Cn^{-1}\\
\leq &\; Cn^{-1}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2},
\end{align*}
where the $\log(n)$ factor accounts for the $O(\log n)$ nonzero $\alpha_{i,j}$ (see \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}), and the constant $Cn^{-1}$ term from the $\partial_{Y_i}$ contribution is absorbed since $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\geq 1$ in the relevant rate regime. The Lipschitz constant of $\E[R_n^{(\delta_n)}|X,U]$ with respect to $\widetilde U$ thus satisfies:
\begin{align*}
\left\|\partial_{\widetilde U}\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\bigg|U,X\right]\right\|_2
\leq C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:gaussian_concentration}:
\begin{align*}
\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X,U\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}
\leq C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Fourth Layer of Randomness: $X_i$}

\noindent We provide the upper bound of
$$\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}.$$
We treat the conditional expectation as a function of $(X_1,\ldots,X_n)$. Consider two realizations that differ only in the $i$-th coordinate, $(X_1,\ldots,X_i,\ldots,X_n)$ and $(X_1,\ldots,X_i^\prime,\ldots,X_n)$. According to Table~\ref{tab:filled_jacobian}, a change from $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ affects $R_n^{(\delta_n)}$ directly through $L_i^{(\delta_n)}$ and indirectly through $\widehat U(S_j)$ for $j\neq i$ with $\alpha_{j,i}\neq 0$.

Let $O_i:=(Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i)$ and $O_i^\prime$ be the observation with $X_i^\prime$. The direct perturbation on $L_i^{(\delta_n)}$ is bounded by Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_bound}:
\begin{align*}
\left|L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(O_i^\prime;\theta,\psi,f,p)\right|
\leq 2\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_i\right| \leq 2C.
\end{align*}
Let $O_j^\prime$ denote the $j$-th observation with updated $\widehat U^\prime(S_j)$ after changing $X_i$. Each indirect perturbation on $L_j^{(\delta_n)}$ is also bounded by $2C$ from Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_bound}. Because the number of non-zero $\alpha_{j,i}$ is of order $\log(n)$, the total perturbation in $R_n^{(\delta_n)}$ from changing $X_i$ to $X_i^\prime$ is bounded by $C\log(n)\,n^{-1}$. Applying McDiarmid's inequality:
\begin{align*}
\left\|\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)|X\right]-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}
\leq C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Sub-Gaussianity:} Combining the four layers:
\begin{align*}
&\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\\
\leq &\underbrace{\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}-\E\left[\cdots|X,U,\xi\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\epsilon$}}
+\underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X,U,\xi\right]-\E\left[\cdots|X,U\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $\xi$}}\\
&+\underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X,U\right]-\E\left[\cdots|X\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $U$}}
+\underbrace{\left\|\E\left[\cdots|X\right]-\E\left[\cdots\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}}_{\text{Randomness in $X$}}\\
\leq &C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2} + C\,n^{-1/2} + C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(n)\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\,n^{-1/2} + C\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\\
\leq &C\left(1+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\right)\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Auxiliary Calculations for Sub-Gaussianity}

\noindent\textbf{Lipschitz Property over $\theta$}
\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_diff_theta}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss  defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} as function of the observed variable. Let $U = (U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$. Let $X = (X_1,\ldots,X_n)$ be the vector of the latent variables in \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1} and \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}. Then, for $\theta,\theta^\prime\in \mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{K}}$, difference of the loss function have the following bounds:
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)\right|\leq C\delta_n^{-1}\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Substituting the formula in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} into the difference of the loss function, and using the fact that the integration limits differ only in the upper bound, we telescope:
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)\\
    = & \underbrace{\int_{\theta^\prime(x,u)}^{\theta(x,u)}(\mathcal{T} - f(a^\prime,x)-u)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime}_{\mathrm{Term\;(I)}}
    + \underbrace{\frac{(y - f(a,x)-u)}{p(a|x,u)}\int_{\theta^\prime(x,u)}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime}_{\mathrm{Term\;(II)}}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Bound on Term (I).} \textcolor{red}{we need to be more careful about the comment of the boundedness, and it needs to be revised later.} 
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|\mathcal{T} - f(a^\prime,x) - u|\leq C^\prime$ uniformly over $(a^\prime,x,u)$. Therefore,
\begin{align*}
    |\mathrm{Term\;(I)}| \leq \sup_{a^\prime,x,u}|\mathcal{T}-f(a^\prime,x)-u|\cdot|\theta(x,u)-\theta^\prime(x,u)| \leq C^\prime\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Bound on Term (II).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|y-f(a,x)-u|\leq C^\prime$ uniformly; by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p(a|x,u)\geq c^\prime>0$. For the kernel integral, since $K_{\delta_n}(t)=K(t/\delta_n)/\delta_n$ and $\kappa:=\|K\|_\infty<\infty$:
\begin{align*}
    \left|\int_{\theta^\prime(x,u)}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime\right|
    \leq |\theta(x,u)-\theta^\prime(x,u)|\cdot\|K_{\delta_n}\|_\infty
    = |\theta(x,u)-\theta^\prime(x,u)|\cdot\frac{\kappa}{\delta_n}.
\end{align*}
Therefore,
\begin{align*}
    |\mathrm{Term\;(II)}| \leq \frac{C^\prime}{c^\prime}\cdot\frac{\kappa}{\delta_n}\cdot\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Combining.}
Adding the two bounds and taking the supremum over $(y,a,u,x)$:
\begin{align*}
    &\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)\right|\\
    \leq &\left(C^\prime + \frac{C^\prime\kappa}{c^\prime\delta_n}\right)\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|_\infty
    \leq \frac{C}{\delta_n}\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|,
\end{align*}
where $C:=C^\prime(1+\kappa/c^\prime)$ is a generic constant, and the last inequality absorbs the $\delta_n^0$ term in Term (I) since $\delta_n\leq 1$ implies $1\leq \delta_n^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y_diff}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss  defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} as function of the observed variable. Let $U = (U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$. Let $X = (X_1,\ldots,X_n)$ be the vector of the latent variables in \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1} and \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}. Then, for $\theta,\theta^\prime\in \mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{K}}$, the difference of the conditional expectation of the derivative of the loss function with respect to the response have the following bound:
       \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{Y_i} \E\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)|U,X\right]\right|\\
        & \leq  C\|\theta-\theta^\prime\| 
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
From the definition \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, $Y_i$ appears only in the IPW term linearly through the numerator $(Y_i - f(A_i,X_i) - \widehat U(S_i))$. The first integral does not involve $Y_i$. Hence,
\begin{align*}
    &\partial_{Y_i}\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)\right]\\
    =\;&\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a - \int_{a_L}^{\theta^\prime(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\right]\\
    =\;&\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\int_{\theta^\prime(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Since this expression does not depend on $Y_i$, the derivative commutes with the conditional expectation:
\begin{align*}
    &\partial_{Y_i}\,\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}(\ldots;\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}(\ldots;\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)\,\Big|\,U,X\right]\\
    =\;&\E\!\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\int_{\theta^\prime(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\;\Bigg|\;U,X\right].
\end{align*}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))\geq c^\prime>0$. By Lemma \ref{lemma:kernel_int_exp_bound},
\begin{align*}
\E\!\left[\int_{\theta^\prime(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\;\Bigg|\;U,X\right]
    =\; C^\prime \|\theta-\theta^\prime\|_\infty\label{eq:kernel_int_diff_exp}
\end{align*}
Thus, we have 
\begin{align*}
    &\left|\partial_{Y_i}\,\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}(\ldots;\theta) - L^{(\delta_n)}(\ldots;\theta^\prime)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|\\
    \leq\;&\frac{1}{c^\prime}\cdot C^\prime\cdot\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|_\infty = \frac{C^\prime}{c^\prime}\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i)$ and setting $C:=C^\prime/c^\prime$ yields the stated bound $C\|\theta-\theta^\prime\|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{L_i_derivative_U_diff}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss  defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} as function of the observed variable. Let $U = (U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$. Let $X = (X_1,\ldots,X_n)$ be the vector of the latent variables in \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1} and \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}. Then, for $\theta,\theta^\prime\in \mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{K}}$, the difference of the loss function have the following bounds:
          \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)} \E\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta^\prime,\psi,f,p)|U,X\right]\right|\\
        & \leq C\cdot \gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty} + C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $u = \widehat{U}(S_i)$ for brevity. From the definition \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, applying the Leibniz rule to differentiate $L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta')$ with respect to $u$, we obtain two terms:
\begin{align*}
&\partial_u\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta')\right]\\
=\;&\underbrace{\left[(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(X_i,u),X_i)-u)\,\partial_u\theta(X_i,u) - (\mathcal{T}-f(\theta'(X_i,u),X_i)-u)\,\partial_u\theta'(X_i,u)\right]}_{\mathrm{Term\ (I)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{Y_i - f(A_i,X_i)-u}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(X_i,u))\,\partial_u\theta(X_i,u) - K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta'(X_i,u))\,\partial_u\theta'(X_i,u)\right]}_{\mathrm{Term\ (II)}}\\
&-\underbrace{\frac{Y_i - f(A_i,X_i)-u}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a + \frac{Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\int_{a_L}^{\theta'(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (III)}}\\
&-\underbrace{\frac{(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u)\,\partial_u p(A_i|X_i,u)}{p^2(A_i|X_i,u)}\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a - \int_{a_L}^{\theta'(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\right]}_{\mathrm{Term\ (IV)}}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Conditional expectation and independence.}
Given $(U,X)$, the integration limits $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ and $\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ are stochastic through $\widehat{U}(S_i)$, which depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ via \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}. The treatment $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$, and $\xi_i \perp \{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ conditionally on $(U,X)$. Hence $A_i$ is conditionally independent of the integration limits given $(U,X)$, and we may factor the conditional expectation.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding $\|\partial_u(\theta-\theta')\|_\infty$ via Landau–Kolmogorov.}
We cannot directly apply Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} to $\theta-\theta'$ and call the result proportional to $\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty$, since the RKHS norm of the difference is not in general controlled by the $L^\infty$ norm.  Instead, we use the Landau–Kolmogorov interpolation inequality:
$$\|\partial_u(\theta-\theta')\|_\infty \leq \sqrt{2\,\|\partial^2_u(\theta-\theta')\|_\infty\cdot\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty}.$$
For the second derivative, applying Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} to $\partial_u(\theta-\theta') \in \mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$ gives $\|\partial^2_u(\theta-\theta')\|_\infty \leq c\,\|\theta-\theta'\|_{\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}}/\gamma_n^2$.  By Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}, $\|\theta-\theta'\|_{\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}} \leq \|\theta\|_{\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}}+\|\theta'\|_{\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}} = O(1/\sqrt{\lambda_n})$, so
$$\|\partial^2_u(\theta-\theta')\|_\infty \leq \frac{c}{\gamma_n^2\sqrt{\lambda_n}}.$$
Substituting back,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LK_derivative}
\|\partial_u(\theta-\theta')\|_\infty \leq c\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty}.
\end{equation}

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (I).}
Adding and subtracting $(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta'(X_i,u),X_i)-u)\partial_u\theta(X_i,u)$, Term (I) decomposes into
\begin{align*}
|\mathrm{Term\ (I)}| \leq\;& \underbrace{\left|\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(X_i,u),X_i)-u\right|}_{\leq\,C'}\cdot\left|\partial_u\theta(X_i,u) - \partial_u\theta'(X_i,u)\right|\\
&+ \underbrace{\left|f(\theta(X_i,u),X_i) - f(\theta'(X_i,u),X_i)\right|}_{\leq\,C'\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty}\cdot\underbrace{\left|\partial_u\theta'(X_i,u)\right|}_{\leq\,C/(\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n})}.
\end{align*}
The first factor of the second sub-term uses Lipschitzness of $f$ (Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}); the second factor uses Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}.  Applying \eqref{eq:LK_derivative} to the first sub-term,
$$|\mathrm{Term\ (I)}| \leq C'\cdot c\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty} + \frac{C'^2}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.$$

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Terms (II)}
For Term (II), decompose the kernel-boundary contribution as
$$K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)\partial_u\theta - K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta')\partial_u\theta' = \underbrace{K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)}_{\text{use Lemma \ref{lemma:kernel_exp_bound}}}\underbrace{(\partial_u\theta-\partial_u\theta')}_{\text{use \eqref{eq:LK_derivative}}} + \underbrace{(K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)-K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta'))}_{\text{use Lemma \ref{lemma:kernel_exp_diff}}}\partial_u\theta'.$$
After taking $\E[\,\cdot\,|U,X]$, the first part gives:
\begin{align*}
&\E\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)(\partial_u\theta-\partial_u\theta')|U,X\right]\\
\leq & \sup\left|(\partial_u\theta-\partial_u\theta')\right| \E\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)|U,X\right]\\
\leq & C'\cdot c\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty}.
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from Lemma \ref{lemma:kernel_exp_bound} and equation \eqref{eq:LK_derivative}.

After taking $\E[\,\cdot\,|U,X]$, the second part gives
\begin{align*}
    &\E\left[(K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)-K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta'))\partial_u\theta'|U,X\right]\\
    \leq & \sup|\partial_u\theta'| \cdot \E\left[(K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)-K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta'))|U,X\right]\\
    \leq &(C^\prime\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty)/(\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n})
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from Lemma \ref{lemma:kernel_exp_diff}, Lemma \ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and Lemma \ref{lemma:bound_svm}. Hence
$$\left|\E[\mathrm{Term\ (II)}|U,X]\right| \leq C_2\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty}+(C^\prime\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty)/(\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}).$$
\noindent\textbf{Bounding Terms (III) and (IV)}
For Terms (III) and (IV), $\int_{a_L}^{\theta}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a - \int_{a_L}^{\theta'}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a = \int_{\theta'}^{\theta}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a$.  By Lemma \ref{lemma:kernel_int_exp_bound},
$$\E\!\left[\int_{\theta'(X_i,u)}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\bigg|\,U,X\right] \leq C'\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.$$
Using $|\partial_u p|\leq C'$ and $p\geq c'$ (Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth},\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}) and the fact that $Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u$ is bounded,  so
$$\left|\E[\mathrm{Term\ (III)}+\mathrm{Term\ (IV)}|U,X]\right| \leq C_3\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.$$

\noindent\textbf{Final assembly.}
Collecting all terms,
$$\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)}\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta')\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|\leq C\cdot \gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty} + C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.$$
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}


\noindent\textbf{Lipschitz Property over $f$}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_diff_f}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}. Then, for $f,f^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_f$, the difference of the loss function satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right| \leq C\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Substituting \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, the difference telescopes in $f$:
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
    =\;&\underbrace{\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}\bigl(f^\prime(a^\prime,x)-f(a^\prime,x)\bigr)\mathrm{d}a^\prime}_{\mathrm{Term\;(I)}}
    +\underbrace{\frac{f^\prime(a,x)-f(a,x)}{p(a|x,u)}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime}_{\mathrm{Term\;(II)}}.
\end{align*}
Note that $\mathcal{T}$ and $u$ are unaffected by the change from $f$ to $f^\prime$.

\noindent\textit{Bound on Term (I).}
Since $|\theta(x,u)-a_L|$ is uniformly bounded by $|\mathcal{A}|$,
\begin{align*}
    |\mathrm{Term\;(I)}|
    \leq \int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}\bigl|f(a^\prime,x)-f^\prime(a^\prime,x)\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime
    \leq |\theta(x,u)-a_L|\cdot\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty
    \leq |\mathcal{A}|\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Bound on Term (II).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p(a|x,u)\geq c^\prime>0$, and since $K_{\delta_n}$ integrates to one, $\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\mathrm{d}a^\prime\leq\int_\R K_{\delta_n}(t)\mathrm{d}t=1$. Therefore,
\begin{align*}
    |\mathrm{Term\;(II)}|
    \leq \frac{|f(a,x)-f^\prime(a,x)|}{c^\prime}
    \leq \frac{\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty}{c^\prime}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Combining.}
Adding the two bounds and taking the supremum over $(y,a,u,x)$,
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\right|
    \leq \left(C^\prime + \frac{1}{c^\prime}\right)\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty
    =: C\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where $C:= |\mathcal{A}|+1/c^\prime$ is a generic constant.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_f}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}. Let $U = (U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$ and $X = (X_1,\ldots,X_n)$ be as in \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}. Then, for $f,f^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_f$ and fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, the difference of the conditional expectation of the $\widehat{U}(S_i)$-derivative of the loss satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)} \E\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f^\prime,p)\big|U,X\right]\right|\\
        &\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $u = \widehat{U}(S_i)$ for brevity.  From the definition \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, the $f,f^\prime$-difference of the loss (for fixed $\theta$) is
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\\
= &\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}\bigl(f^\prime(a,X_i)-f(a,X_i)\bigr)\mathrm{d}a
- \frac{f^\prime(A_i,X_i)-f(A_i,X_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Since $f$ and $f^\prime$ do not depend on $u$, applying the Leibniz rule to differentiate with respect to $u$ yields three terms:
\begin{align*}
&\partial_u\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\\
=\;&\underbrace{\bigl(f^\prime(\theta(X_i,u),X_i)-f(\theta(X_i,u),X_i)\bigr)\cdot\frac{\partial\theta(X_i,u)}{\partial u}}_{\mathrm{Term\ (I)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{f(A_i,X_i)-f^\prime(A_i,X_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\cdot K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta(X_i,u)\right)\cdot\frac{\partial\theta(X_i,u)}{\partial u}}_{\mathrm{Term\ (II)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{f^\prime(A_i,X_i)-f(A_i,X_i)}{p^2(A_i|X_i,u)}\cdot\frac{\partial p(A_i|X_i,u)}{\partial u}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (III)}},
\end{align*}
where Term (I) arises from differentiating the upper limit of the first integral, Term (II) from differentiating the upper limit of $\int K$ in the IPW term, and Term (III) from differentiating $1/p$ via the quotient rule.

\noindent\textbf{Conditional expectation and independence.}
Given $(U,X)$, $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ while $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$; since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ given $(U,X)$, we may factor conditional expectations of products involving $A_i$ and $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (I).}
Taking $\E[\,\cdot\,|U,X]$ and bounding pointwise,
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (I)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq \|f-f^\prime\|_\infty\cdot\sup_u\left|\frac{\partial\theta(X_i,u)}{\partial u}\right|
\leq \frac{C}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where the last step uses Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (II).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p\geq c^\prime>0$.  Factoring out the nonstochastic quantities,
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (II)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
&\leq \frac{\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty}{c^\prime}\cdot\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta(X_i,u)\right)\Big|U,X\right]\cdot\sup_u\left|\frac{\partial\theta(X_i,u)}{\partial u}\right|\\
&\leq \frac{C'\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty}{c^\prime}\cdot C\cdot\frac{1}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}
= \frac{CC^\prime}{c^\prime\,\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where we applied Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_theta_exp_bound} to bound $\E[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta)|U,X]\leq C^\prime$, and Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} together with Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm} to bound $\sup_u|\partial_u\theta|\leq C/(\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n})$.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (III).}
By the quotient rule bound $p^{-2}\leq c^{\prime-2}$ and Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|\partial_u p|\leq C^\prime$.  Hence,
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (III)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
&\leq \frac{C^\prime\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty}{c^{\prime 2}}\cdot\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\Bigg|U,X\right]\\
&\leq \frac{C^\prime C}{c^{\prime 2}}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where we applied Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound} to bound the conditional kernel integral by the constant $C$.

\noindent\textbf{Final assembly.}
Collecting the three bounds and noting that $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\geq 1$ for the relevant rate regime (so that the constant from Term (III) is absorbed),
$$\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)}\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty.$$
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}


\noindent\textbf{Lipschitz Property over $p$}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_diff_p}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}. Then, for $p,p^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_p$, $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$, the difference of the loss function satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right| \leq C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $p$ appears only in the denominator of the IPW term in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, the first integral (which does not involve $p$) cancels exactly, leaving a single-term difference:
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\\
    =\;&\left(y-f(a,x)-u\right)\left[\frac{1}{p(a|x,u)}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(a|x,u)}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime.
\end{align*}
Writing the difference of reciprocals as $\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^\prime} = \frac{p^\prime-p}{p\cdot p^\prime}$ and bounding each factor pointwise: $|y-f(a,x)-u|\leq C^\prime$ by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}; $p,p^\prime\geq c^\prime>0$ by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}; and $\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\mathrm{d}a^\prime\leq\int_\R K_{\delta_n}(t)\mathrm{d}t=1$, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\right|
    \leq \frac{C^\prime}{c^{\prime 2}}\left|p(a|x,u)-p^\prime(a|x,u)\right|
    \leq \frac{C^\prime}{c^{\prime 2}}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
Taking the supremum over $(y,a,u,x)$ and setting $C:=C^\prime/c^{\prime 2}$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_p}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}. Let $U = (U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$ and $X = (X_1,\ldots,X_n)$ be as in \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}. Then, for $p,p^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_p$ and fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, the difference of the conditional expectation of the $\widehat{U}(S_i)$-derivative of the loss satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)} \E\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p^\prime)\big|U,X\right]\right|\\
        &\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $u = \widehat{U}(S_i)$ for brevity.  From the definition \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}, the $p,p^\prime$-difference of the loss (for fixed $\theta$, $f$) is
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\\
= &\left(Y_i - f(A_i,X_i) - u\right)\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,u)}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Applying the Leibniz rule to differentiate with respect to $u$ yields three terms:
\begin{align*}
&\partial_u\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\\
=\;&\underbrace{\left(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u\right)\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,u)}\right]K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(X_i,u))\frac{\partial\theta(X_i,u)}{\partial u}}_{\mathrm{Term\ (I)}}\\
&+\underbrace{(-1)\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,u)}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (II)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\left(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u\right)\partial_u\!\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,u)}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (III)}},
\end{align*}
where Term (I) arises from differentiating the upper limit $\theta(X_i,u)$ of $\int K$, Term (II) from differentiating the residual factor $(Y_i-f-u)$ with respect to $u$ (yielding $-1$), and Term (III) from differentiating $[1/p-1/p^\prime]$ via the quotient rule.

\noindent\textbf{Conditional expectation and independence.}
Given $(U,X)$, $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ while $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$; since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ given $(U,X)$, we may factor conditional expectations of products involving $A_i$ and $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (I).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u|\leq C^\prime$.  Writing $|1/p - 1/p^\prime|=(p^\prime-p)/(pp^\prime)\leq \|p-p^\prime\|_\infty/c^{\prime 2}$ by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap},
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (I)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
&\leq \frac{C^\prime\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty}{c^{\prime 2}}\cdot\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(X_i,u))\,\big|\,U,X\right]\cdot\sup_u\left|\frac{\partial\theta(X_i,u)}{\partial u}\right|\\
