&\leq \frac{C^\prime\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty}{c^{\prime 2}}\cdot C\cdot\frac{C}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}},
\end{align*}
where we applied Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_theta_exp_bound} and Lemmas~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm}--\ref{lemma:bound_svm}.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (II).}
By the same $|1/p-1/p^\prime|\leq\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty/c^{\prime 2}$ bound and Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound},
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (II)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
&\leq \frac{\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty}{c^{\prime 2}}\cdot\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\Bigg|U,X\right]
\leq \frac{C}{c^{\prime 2}}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (III).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|\partial_u p|,|\partial_u p^\prime|\leq C^\prime$, so
$$\left|\partial_u\!\left[\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^\prime}\right]\right| = \left|\frac{-\partial_u p}{p^2}+\frac{\partial_u p^\prime}{p^{\prime 2}}\right|\leq \frac{2C^\prime}{c^{\prime 2}}.$$
Combined with $|Y_i-f-u|\leq C^\prime$ and Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound},
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (III)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
&\leq \frac{2C^{\prime 2}}{c^{\prime 2}}\cdot\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\Bigg|U,X\right]
\leq \frac{2C^{\prime 2}C}{c^{\prime 2}}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Final assembly.}
Collecting the three bounds and noting that $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\geq 1$ for the relevant rate regime (so that the constant terms from Terms (II) and (III) are absorbed into the dominant $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$ factor),
$$\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)}\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.$$
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y_diff_p}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}. Then, for $p,p^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_p$ and fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$,
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\E\!\left[\partial_{Y_i}\!\left\{L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right\}\Big|U,X\right]\right|
        \leq C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
From Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_p}, the $p,p^\prime$-difference of the loss is
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\\
= &\left(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)\right)\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta}K_{\delta_n}\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Differentiating with respect to $Y_i$ (noting that $Y_i$ enters only through the residual factor $(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i))$),
\begin{align*}
&\partial_{Y_i}\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\\
= &\left[\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}-\frac{1}{p^\prime(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\right]\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Taking the conditional expectation given $(U,X)$ and bounding each factor: $|1/p-1/p^\prime|\leq\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty/c^{\prime 2}$ by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, and
$$\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\Bigg|U,X\right]\leq C$$
by Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound}.  Therefore,
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\partial_{Y_i}\!\left\{L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right\}\Big|U,X\right]\right|
\leq \frac{C}{c^{\prime 2}}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty =: C\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty.
\end{align*}
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{Lipschitz Property over $\psi$}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, where $\widehat U(S_i)=\widehat U(S_i,\psi)$ is computed from the precision matrix $\Lambda_n(\psi)=\Sigma_n(\psi)^{-1}$ via \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}. Let $\widehat U^\prime(S_i)=\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)$ be the corresponding estimate under $\psi^\prime$. Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and \ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap},
    \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|\\
        \leq &  C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\sqrt{\log(n)}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in two steps.

\noindent\textbf{Step 1: Lipschitz bound of the loss in $\widehat U(S_i)$.}

From \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, $\widehat U(S_i)$ enters the loss through (a) the integration limits $\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$, (b) the integrand $\mathcal{T}-f(a,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)$, (c) the residual numerator $Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)$, and (d) the propensity score denominator $p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$. Specifically, the difference of the los function can be written as
\begin{align*}
    &L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\\
 = & \int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}(\widehat U^\prime(S_i)-\widehat U(S_i))\mathrm{d}a \\
 & + \int_{\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}(\mathcal{T}-f(a,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i))\mathrm{d}a\\
 & + \left(\frac{Y_i - f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U^\prime(S_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}- \frac{Y_i - f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\right)\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))} K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\mathrm{d}a\\
 & + \frac{Y_i - f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\int_{\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))} K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\mathrm{d}a
\end{align*}



By Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} together with Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}, $\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$ is uniformly Lipschitz with $\widehat U(S_i)$ with constant $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$. By Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, other three terms are uniformly Lipschitz in $u$ with a constant $C$. Therefore, applying the mean value theorem,
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{Y_i,A_i,X_i}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\right|
    \leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2} \delta_n^{-1}\sup_i\left|\widehat U(S_i,\psi) - \widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)\right|.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Step 2: Bounding Diagonal Entry and Spectral Norm of precision Matrix.} Let $\Lambda=\Lambda_n(\psi)$, $\Lambda^\prime=\Lambda_n(\psi^\prime)$, $\Lambda_{ii}=e_i^\top\Lambda e_i$, and $\widehat{R}=(\widehat R_1,\ldots,\widehat R_n)^\top$ with $\widehat R_j$ defined in \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}. First of all, we show that there exists constants $C>c>0$ such that $0<c<\Lambda_{ii},\Lambda^\prime_{ii}<C$ for every $i$ and both $\|\Lambda\|_{\text{op}}$ and $\|\Lambda^\prime\|_{\text{op}}<C$. The diagonal entries of the precision matrix are bounded below as:
\begin{align*}
    \Lambda_{ii} =& 1/\text{Var}(U(S_i)+\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\\
    \geq & 1/\text{Var}(U(S_i)+\epsilon_i)\\
    = & 1/(\sigma_\epsilon^{2} +\sigma_{\text{gp}}^{2})
\end{align*}
The diagonal entries of the precision matrix are bounded above as:
\begin{align*}
    \Lambda_{ii} =& 1/\text{Var}(U(S_i)+\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\\
    = & 1/\left\{\text{Var}(U(S_i)|U_{-i})+\text{Var}(\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\right\}\\
    \leq & 1/\text{Var}(\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\\
    = & 1/\text{Var}(\epsilon_i) = \sigma_\epsilon^2
\end{align*}
Additionally, the spectral norm of the precision matrix is bounded above as:\begin{align*}
\|\Lambda\|_{\mathrm{op}}=&\|\Sigma^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    = (\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma))^{-1}
    \leq 1/\sigma^2_\epsilon.
\end{align*}
Here, $\sigma_\epsilon^{2}$ and $\sigma_{gp}^{2}$ are the coefficients of the nugget effect variance corresponding to the covariance parameter $\psi$. 


\noindent\textbf{Step 3: Bounding $\sup_i|\widehat U(S_i,\psi)-\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)|$ by $\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$.} From the definition $\alpha_{ij}=-\Lambda_{ii}^{-1}\Lambda_{ij}$ and $\widehat U(S_i,\psi)=\sum_{j\neq i}\alpha_{ij}\widehat R_j$, a direct computation gives
$$\widehat U(S_i,\psi) = \widehat R_i - \frac{e_i^\top \Lambda\,\widehat{R}}{e_i^\top\Lambda e_i}.$$
Hence the difference is
\begin{align*}
\Delta\widehat U_i := \widehat U(S_i,\psi) - \widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)
= \frac{e_i^\top\Lambda^\prime\widehat{R}}{\Lambda^\prime_{ii}} - \frac{e_i^\top\Lambda\widehat{R}}{\Lambda_{ii}}.
\end{align*}
Adding and subtracting the cross-term $e_i^\top\Lambda^\prime\widehat{R}/\Lambda_{ii}$ splits the perturbation into denominator and numerator parts:
\begin{align*}
\Delta\widehat U_i
= \underbrace{(e_i^\top\Lambda^\prime\widehat{R})\cdot\frac{\Lambda_{ii}-\Lambda^\prime_{ii}}{\Lambda_{ii}\Lambda^\prime_{ii}}}_{\text{denominator perturbation}}
+ \underbrace{\frac{e_i^\top(\Lambda^\prime-\Lambda)\widehat{R}}{\Lambda_{ii}}}_{\text{numerator perturbation}}.
\end{align*}
Taking absolute values and applying Cauchy--Schwarz ($|x^\top y|\leq\|x\|_2\|y\|_2$) and the operator norm bound ($\|Ax\|_2\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|x\|_2$):
\begin{align*}
|\Delta\widehat U_i|
&\leq \|\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{R}_{N(i)}\|_2\cdot\frac{|\Lambda_{ii}-\Lambda^\prime_{ii}|}{\Lambda_{ii}\Lambda^\prime_{ii}}
+ \frac{\|\Lambda^\prime-\Lambda\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{R}_{N(i)}\|_2}{\Lambda_{ii}},
\end{align*}
where $\widehat{R}_{N(i)}$ denotes the subvector of $\widehat{R}$ restricted to the $O(\log n)$ nonzero neighbours of $i$ in the Vecchia approximation \eqref{eq:vecchina_approximation_nonzero}, so $\|\widehat{R}_{N(i)}\|_2=O(\sqrt{\log n})$ is bounded. Since $|\Lambda_{ii}-\Lambda^\prime_{ii}|=|e_i^\top(\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime)e_i|\leq\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}$, we factor:
\begin{align*}
|\Delta\widehat U_i|
\leq \|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|\widehat{R}_{N(i)}\|_2\left(\frac{\|\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\Lambda_{ii}\Lambda^\prime_{ii}}+\frac{1}{\Lambda_{ii}}\right)
\leq C\sqrt{\log(n)}\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\end{align*}
where the last inequality absorbs the term related to $\Lambda_{ii},\Lambda^\prime_{ii},\|\Lambda^\prime\|_{\text{op}}$ into the constant $C$ by the boundedness condition we showed at the beginning of this step. Finally, using the identity $\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime=\Sigma^{-1}(\Sigma^\prime-\Sigma)(\Sigma^\prime)^{-1}$ and sub-multiplicativity of the operator norm:
$$\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\Sigma_n(\psi)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\,\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.$$ Then, taking $\sup_i$ and combining with Step~1 yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$\ell_2$-Norm Bound on $\widehat{U}$ Perturbation]\label{lemma:widehat_U_diff_psi_L2}
Let $\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi):=(\widehat{U}(S_1,\psi),\ldots,\widehat{U}(S_n,\psi))^\top$ and $\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi^\prime):=(\widehat{U}(S_1,\psi^\prime),\ldots,\widehat{U}(S_n,\psi^\prime))^\top$ be computed via \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i} with precision matrices $\Lambda_n(\psi)=\Sigma_n(\psi)^{-1}$ and $\Lambda_n(\psi^\prime)=\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)^{-1}$, respectively. Let $\widehat{\mathbf{R}}=(\widehat{R}_1,\ldots,\widehat{R}_n)^\top$ with $\widehat{R}_j=Y_j-f(A_j,X_j)$.  Under Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth},
\begin{align*}
\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi)-\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi^\prime)\|_2
\leq & C\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2\\
\leq & C\sqrt{n}\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\noindent\textbf{Step 1: Matrix representation of $\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi)$.}

From $\widehat U(S_i,\psi)=\widehat R_i - e_i^\top\Lambda\widehat{\mathbf{R}}/\Lambda_{ii}$ (see the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}), the full vector satisfies
\begin{align*}
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi) = \widehat{\mathbf{R}} - D(\Lambda)^{-1}\Lambda\,\widehat{\mathbf{R}} = M(\Lambda)\,\widehat{\mathbf{R}},
\qquad M(\Lambda):=I - D(\Lambda)^{-1}\Lambda,
\end{align*}
where $\Lambda:=\Lambda_n(\psi)$ and $D(\Lambda):=\mathrm{diag}(\Lambda_{11},\ldots,\Lambda_{nn})$.  Note that $[M(\Lambda)]_{ii}=1-\Lambda_{ii}/\Lambda_{ii}=0$, confirming that $\widehat U(S_i,\psi)$ does not depend on $\widehat{R}_i$.  Similarly, $\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi^\prime)=M(\Lambda^\prime)\widehat{\mathbf{R}}$ with $\Lambda^\prime:=\Lambda_n(\psi^\prime)$.

\noindent\textbf{Step 2: Bounding $\|M(\Lambda^\prime)-M(\Lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$.}

The matrix difference is
\begin{align*}
M(\Lambda^\prime)-M(\Lambda) = D(\Lambda)^{-1}\Lambda - D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}\Lambda^\prime
= \underbrace{D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}(\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime)}_{\text{numerator perturbation}}
+ \underbrace{(D(\Lambda)^{-1}-D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1})\Lambda}_{\text{denominator perturbation}}.
\end{align*}
\emph{Numerator perturbation.}
Since the diagonal entries of the precision matrix are bounded below as:
\begin{align*}
    \Lambda_{ii} =& 1/\text{Var}(U(S_i)+\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\\
    \geq & 1/\text{Var}(U(S_i)+\epsilon_i)\\
    = & 1/(\sigma_\epsilon^{2} +\sigma_{\text{gp}}^{2})
\end{align*}
where $\sigma_\epsilon^{2}$ and $\sigma_{gp}^{2}$ are the coefficients of the nugget effect variance corresponding to the covariance parameter $\psi$. Thus, we have
$\|D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C$ for some positive constant $C$.  Hence
$$\|D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}(\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \|D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}.$$
\emph{Denominator perturbation.}
$D(\Lambda)^{-1}-D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}$ is diagonal with $i$-th entry $\Lambda_{ii}^{-1}-(\Lambda_{ii}^\prime)^{-1}=(\Lambda_{ii}^\prime-\Lambda_{ii})/(\Lambda_{ii}\Lambda_{ii}^\prime)$. In addition to be bounded below, diagonal entries of the precision matrix are above as:
\begin{align*}
    \Lambda_{ii} =& 1/\text{Var}(U(S_i)+\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\\
    = & 1/\left\{\text{Var}(U(S_i)|U_{-i})+\text{Var}(\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\right\}\\
    \leq & 1/\text{Var}(\epsilon_i|U_{-i})\\
    = & 1/\text{Var}(\epsilon_i) = \sigma_\epsilon^2
\end{align*}
where $\sigma_\epsilon^{2}$ is the coefficients of the nugget effect variance corresponding to the covariance parameter $\psi$. So,
$$\|D(\Lambda)^{-1}-D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = \max_i\frac{|\Lambda_{ii}-\Lambda_{ii}^\prime|}{\Lambda_{ii}\Lambda_{ii}^\prime}\leq C\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}},$$
since $|\Lambda_{ii}-\Lambda_{ii}^\prime|\leq\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ and the denominators are bounded below. Combined with 
\begin{align*}
\|\Lambda\|_{\mathrm{op}}=&\|\Sigma^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
    =& (\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma))^{-1}\\
    \leq & 1/\sigma^2_\epsilon.
\end{align*}
We then have
$$\|(D(\Lambda)^{-1}-D(\Lambda^\prime)^{-1})\Lambda\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}.$$
Adding the two parts,
$$\|M(\Lambda^\prime)-M(\Lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}.$$

\noindent\textbf{Step 3: Translating to $\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$.}

Applying the operator norm and using the identity $\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime=\Sigma^{-1}(\Sigma^\prime-\Sigma)(\Sigma^\prime)^{-1}$ as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi},
\begin{align*}
\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi)-\widehat{\mathbf{U}}(\psi^\prime)\|_2
= &\|(M(\Lambda^\prime)-M(\Lambda))\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2
\leq \|M(\Lambda^\prime)-M(\Lambda)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2\\
\leq & C\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2\\
\leq &C\|\Sigma_n(\psi)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2\\
& C \|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Bounding $\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2$.}

Since $\widehat{R}_j=Y_j-f(A_j,X_j)$ and $|Y_j-f(A_j,X_j)|\leq C^\prime$ uniformly by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, we have $\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\|_2\leq C^\prime\sqrt{n}$.  Substituting yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y_diff_psi}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, where $\widehat U(S_i)=\widehat U(S_i,\psi)$ and $\widehat U^\prime(S_i)=\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)$.  Let $U=(U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$ and $X=(X_1,\ldots,X_n)$.  Then, for $\psi,\psi^\prime$ and fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$, $p\in\mathcal{F}_p$, the derivative of the conditional expectation of the loss difference with respect to the response satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{Y_i}\,\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi^\prime,f,p)\,\Big|\,U,X\right]\right|\\
        &\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(n)}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\noindent\textbf{Identifying the $Y_i$-dependent terms.}

From the decomposition in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi} (equation \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}), the $\psi,\psi^\prime$-difference of the loss expands into four terms:
\begin{align*}
&L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\\
=\;&\underbrace{\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}(\widehat U^\prime(S_i)-\widehat U(S_i))\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term (1): no }Y_i}
+\underbrace{\int_{\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}(\mathcal{T}-f(a,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i))\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term (2): no }Y_i}\\
&+\underbrace{\left(\frac{Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U^\prime(S_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}-\frac{Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\right)\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term (3): contains }Y_i}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i)}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\int_{\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term (4): contains }Y_i}.
\end{align*}
Terms (1) and (2) do not involve $Y_i$, so they contribute zero to $\partial_{Y_i}$.  Differentiating Terms (3) and (4) with respect to $Y_i$:
\begin{align*}
&\partial_{Y_i}\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\\
=\;&\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}-\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\right)\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (A)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\int_{\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (B)}}.
\end{align*}
Since neither term depends on $Y_i$, the derivative commutes with the conditional expectation:
$$\partial_{Y_i}\,\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\psi)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\psi^\prime)\,\big|\,U,X\right] = \E\!\left[\partial_{Y_i}\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\psi)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\psi^\prime)\right]\Big|\,U,X\right].$$

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (A).}

Writing the difference of reciprocal propensities as
$$\left|\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U^\prime)}-\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U)}\right| = \frac{|p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U)-p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U^\prime)|}{p\cdot p^\prime}\leq \frac{C^\prime|\Delta\widehat U_i|}{c^{\prime 2}},$$
where we used the Lipschitz bound $|p(\cdot|x,u)-p(\cdot|x,u^\prime)|\leq C^\prime|u-u^\prime|$ from Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and $p,p^\prime\geq c^\prime>0$ from Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}.  Since $\int_{a_L}^{\theta}K_{\delta_n}\,\mathrm{d}a\leq \int_\R K_{\delta_n}\,\mathrm{d}a = 1$,
$$\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (A)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right| \leq \frac{C^\prime}{c^{\prime 2}}\,\E\!\left[|\Delta\widehat U_i|\,\big|\,U,X\right].$$
By the pathwise bound $|\Delta\widehat U_i|\leq C\sqrt{\log(n)}\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ established in Step~3 of Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}, the operator-norm identity $\|\Lambda-\Lambda^\prime\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\Sigma_n(\psi)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$, and the boundedness of $\|\Sigma_n(\psi)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ established in Step 2 in Lemma \ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}, 
\begin{align}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (A)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq C\sqrt{\log(n)}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.\label{eq:termA_psi}
\end{align}

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (B).}

By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p\geq c^\prime>0$.  By the same conditional-independence and Fubini argument as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_exp_bound} — with integration limits $\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))$ and $\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))$ in place of $\theta^\prime$ and $\theta$ — we obtain
$$\E\!\left[\int_{\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime)}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\;\Bigg|\;U,X\right]\leq C^\prime\,\E\!\left[|\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))-\theta(X_i,\widehat U^\prime(S_i))|\,\big|\,U,X\right].$$
Applying the Lipschitz bound $|\theta(x,u)-\theta(x,u^\prime)|\leq \sup_u|\partial_u\theta|\cdot|u-u^\prime|\leq\frac{C}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}|u-u^\prime|$ (Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}) and the pathwise bound on $|\Delta\widehat U_i|$,
\begin{align}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (B)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
&\leq \frac{1}{c^\prime}\cdot\frac{CC^\prime}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}\,\E\!\left[|\Delta\widehat U_i|\,\big|\,U,X\right]\nonumber\\
&\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(n)}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.\label{eq:termB_psi}
\end{align}

\noindent\textbf{Final assembly.}

Adding \eqref{eq:termA_psi} and \eqref{eq:termB_psi} and noting that $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\geq 1$ in the relevant rate regime (so Term (A) is absorbed into the Term (B) bound),
$$\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{Y_i}\,\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\psi)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\psi^\prime)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(n)}\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.$$
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_U_diff_psi}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, where $\widehat U(S_i)=\widehat U(S_i,\psi)$ and $\widehat U^\prime(S_i)=\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)$. Let $U=(U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$ and $X=(X_1,\ldots,X_n)$. Then, for $\psi,\psi^\prime$ and fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$, $p\in\mathcal{F}_p$, the derivative of the conditional expectation of the loss difference with respect to $\widehat U(S_i,\psi)$ satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\E\!\left[\partial_{\widehat U(S_i,\psi)}\,L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p) - \partial_{\widehat U(S_i,\psi^\prime)}\,L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\,\Big|\,U,X\right]\right|\\
        &\leq C\,(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(n)}\,\,\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $u=\widehat{U}(S_i,\psi)$, $u^\prime=\widehat{U}(S_i,\psi^\prime)$, and $\Delta:=\Delta\widehat{U}_i=u-u^\prime$ (treated as a fixed offset so that $u^\prime=u-\Delta$). Setting $F(u):=L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi;u)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime;u-\Delta)$, the $\psi$,$\psi^\prime$-difference $F(u)$ has the four-term decomposition of Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}:
\begin{align*}
F(u)
=\;&\underbrace{-\Delta\bigl(\theta(X_i,u)-a_L\bigr)}_{\text{Term 1}}
+\underbrace{\int_{\theta(X_i,u^\prime)}^{\theta(X_i,u)}\!\bigl(\mathcal{T}-f(a,X_i)-u\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term 2}}\\
&+\underbrace{\Bigl(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u^\prime}{p^\prime}-\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\Bigr)\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term 3}}\\
&+\underbrace{\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\int_{\theta(X_i,u^\prime)}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\text{Term 4}},
\end{align*}
where $p=p(A_i|X_i,u)$, $p^\prime=p(A_i|X_i,u^\prime)$, $\theta=\theta(X_i,u)$, $\theta^\prime=\theta(X_i,u^\prime)$. Since $u^\prime=u-\Delta$ moves with $u$ (i.e.\ $\mathrm{d}u^\prime/\mathrm{d}u=1$), the Leibniz rule gives $F^\prime(u)=\mathrm{d}F/\mathrm{d}u$ with contributions from each term.

\noindent\textbf{Conditional expectation and independence.}
Given $(U,X)$, $\theta(X_i,u)$ depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ via $u=\widehat{U}(S_i,\psi)$, while $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$. Since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ given $(U,X)$ by the data-generating model, we may factor conditional expectations of products involving $A_i$ and integration limits.

\noindent\textbf{Term (I): derivative of Term 1.}
Differentiating $-\Delta(\theta(X_i,u)-a_L)$ with respect to $u$ (with $\Delta$ fixed) gives $-\Delta\,\partial_u\theta(X_i,u)$. Hence
$$\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\,(I)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|\leq|\Delta|\cdot\sup_u|\partial_u\theta|\leq\frac{C|\Delta|}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}},$$
where we applied Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}.

\noindent\textbf{Term (II): derivative of Term 2.}
Since $u^\prime=u-\Delta$ also varies with $u$, the Leibniz rule applied to $\int_{\theta(u^\prime)}^{\theta(u)}(\mathcal{T}-f(a)-u)\,\mathrm{d}a$ yields
\begin{align*}
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\,\mathrm{Term\;2}
=\;&\bigl(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(u))-u\bigr)\partial_u\theta(u)
-\bigl(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(u^\prime))-u\bigr)\partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)
-\bigl(\theta(X_i,u)-\theta(X_i,u^\prime)\bigr).
\end{align*}
The last sub-term satisfies $|\theta(u)-\theta(u^\prime)|\leq\sup|\partial_u\theta|\cdot|\Delta|\leq C|\Delta|/(\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n})$ by Lipschitz continuity of $\theta$ (Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}). For the first two sub-terms, 
\begin{align*}
    & \bigl(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(u))-u\bigr)\partial_u\theta(u)
-\bigl(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(u^\prime))-u\bigr)\partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)\\
= & \bigl(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(u))-u\bigr)\{\partial_u\theta(u) - \partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)\}+ \{f(\theta(u)) - f(\theta(u^\prime))\}\partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)\\
\leq & C(\gamma_n^{-1}+\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta|
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from (i) By Lemma \ref{lemma:bound_svm} and Lemma \ref{lemma:derivative_svm}, $\partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)\leq \gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$; (ii) By Assumption \ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, we have $f(\theta(u)) - f(\theta(u^\prime))\leq |\Delta|$ and $\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(u))-u\leq C$ (iii) Also, $\partial_u\theta(u) - \partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)\leq \gamma_n^{-2}\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta|$ from Lemma Lemma \ref{lemma:bound_svm} and Lemma \ref{lemma:derivative_svm}. Hence
$$\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\,(II)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|\leq\frac{C|\Delta|}{\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}}+\frac{C|\Delta|}{\gamma_n^2\sqrt{\lambda_n}}.$$

\noindent\textbf{Term (III): derivative of Term 3.}
Applying the product rule to $\bigl(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u^\prime}{p^\prime}-\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\bigr)\int_{a_L}^{\theta(u)}K\,\mathrm{d}a$, the derivative w.r.t.\ $u$ (with $u^\prime=u-\Delta$) produces two parts:
\begin{align*}
\text{Part (a):}&\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\Bigl(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u^\prime}{p^\prime}-\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\Bigr)\cdot\int_{a_L}^{\theta(u)}K\,\mathrm{d}a,\\
\text{Part (b):}&\quad\Bigl(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u^\prime}{p^\prime}-\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\Bigr)\cdot K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(u))\,\partial_u\theta(u).
\end{align*}
For Part (a): differentiating each fraction with $\mathrm{d}u^\prime/\mathrm{d}u=1$ gives 
\begin{align*}
    &\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u^\prime}{p^\prime}-\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p})\\
    =&(\tfrac{1}{p}-\tfrac{1}{p^\prime})- \tfrac{1}{p^{\prime2}}\tfrac{\mathrm{d p^\prime}}{\mathrm{d}u} +\tfrac{1}{p^{2}}\tfrac{\mathrm{d p}}{\mathrm{d}u} \\
    \leq & C|\Delta|
\end{align*}
 by Lipschitz of $p$ and $\partial_u p$ in Assumption \ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}. Combined with Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound}, we have $$\left|\E[\mathrm{Part\,(a)}|U,X]\right|\leq C|\Delta|.$$
For Part (b): first, Assumption \ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}(Lipschitz), we have
$$|\tfrac{Y_i-f-u^\prime}{p^\prime}-\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}|\leq C|\Delta|.$$ Applying Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_theta_exp_bound} (boundedness of the conditional expectation of the surrogate kernel) and Lemma~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} plus \ref{lemma:bound_svm} (boundedness of SVM and its derivatives), we have $$\left|\E[\mathrm{Part\,(b)}|U,X]\right|\leq C|\Delta|\cdot C\cdot C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.$$
Hence $\left|\E[\mathrm{Term\,(III)}|U,X]\right|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta|$ (since $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\geq 1$ absorbs Part (a)).

\noindent\textbf{Term (IV): derivative of Term 4.}
Applying the product rule to $\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\int_{\theta(u^\prime)}^{\theta(u)}K\,\mathrm{d}a$:
\begin{align*}
\text{Part (c):}&\quad\partial_u\!\Bigl(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\Bigr)\int_{\theta(u^\prime)}^{\theta(u)}K\,\mathrm{d}a,\\
\text{Part (d):}&\quad\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}\,\bigl[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(u))\partial_u\theta(u)-K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(u^\prime))\partial_{u^\prime}\theta(u^\prime)\bigr].
\end{align*}
For Part (c): By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth},~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, we have 
$$|\partial_u(\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p})|\leq C.$$
Then, by the boundedness of the derivative of the kernel SVM in Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_exp_bound}, we have $$\E[\int_{\theta(u^\prime)}^{\theta(u)}K\,\mathrm{d}a|U,X]\leq C|\theta(u)-\theta(u^\prime)|\leq C|\Delta|/(\gamma_n\sqrt{\lambda_n}).$$
For Part (d): By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth},~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, we have
$$|\tfrac{Y_i-f-u}{p}|\leq C^\prime.$$ 
Then, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_theta_exp_bound}, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\E[|K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(u))-K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(u^\prime))||U,X]\\
    \leq & C|\theta(u)-\theta(u^\prime)|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta|
\end{align*}
together with $|\partial_u\theta|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$, $\left|\E[\mathrm{Part\,(d)}|U,X]\right|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta|$.
Hence $\left|\E[\mathrm{Term\,(IV)}|U,X]\right|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta|$.

\noindent\textbf{Final assembly.}
Collecting Terms (I)--(IV), each bounded by $C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta\widehat{U}_i|$,
$$\left|\partial_{\widehat{U}(S_i,\psi)}\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p)-L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|\leq C(\gamma_n^{-1} +\gamma_n^{-2})\lambda_n^{-1/2}|\Delta\widehat{U}_i|.$$
Applying the bound of $|\Delta \widehat U_i|$ in Step 3 of Lemma~\ref{lemma:L_i_diff_psi}, and taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$, yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Bound on Conditional Kernel Expectation]\label{lemma:kernel_exp_bound}
Let $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$ and let $p(a|X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ denote the conditional density of $A_i$ given $(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$. Under Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, there exists a constant $C'>0$ such that
$$\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i - \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\bigg|U,X\right] \leq C'.$$
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Conditioning on $(U,X)$, the policy evaluation $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ is stochastic through $\widehat{U}(S_i)$, which depends on $\tilde\varepsilon_i := \sum_{j\neq i}\alpha_{ij}\varepsilon_j$ via \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}.  The treatment $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$, and $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ conditionally on $(U,X)$ by the data-generating model \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}.  Hence $A_i$ and $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ are conditionally independent given $(U,X)$.

Let $p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\cdot)$ denote the density of $\tilde\varepsilon_i$ given $(U,X)$, and let $p_A(a) := p(a|X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$.  By the conditional independence and Fubini's theorem,
\begin{align*}
&\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\bigg|U,X\right]\\
= \;&\int_{\R}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{A}}\delta_n^{-1}K\!\left(\frac{a-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}{\delta_n}\right)p_A(a)\,\mathrm{d}a\right\}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon.
\end{align*}
Substituting $v = (a - \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))/\delta_n$ in the inner integral,
\begin{align*}
= \;&\int_{\R}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{A}}K(v)\,p_A\!\left(\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))+\delta_n v\right)\mathrm{d}v\right\}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon\\
\leq \;&\sup_{a}p_A(a)\int_{\R}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{A}}K(v)\,\mathrm{d}v\right\}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon
= \sup_{a}p_A(a) =: C',
\end{align*}
where the last equality uses $\int K(v)\,\mathrm{d}v=1$ and $\int p_{\tilde\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon=1$, and $C'$ is finite by the bounded density assumption (Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bound on Conditional Kernel Difference Expectation]\label{lemma:kernel_exp_diff}
Let $\theta,\theta'\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$.  Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and \ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, there exists a constant $C'>0$ such that
$$\left|\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right) - K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\bigg|U,X\right]\right| \leq C'\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.$$
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By the same conditional independence argument as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_exp_bound}, $A_i$ and $(\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)),\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))$ are jointly conditionally independent of $A_i$ given $(U,X)$.  Applying Fubini and the substitution $v=(a-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))/\delta_n$ in the first kernel and $v=(a-\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))/\delta_n$ in the second,
\begin{align*}
&\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right) - K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\bigg|U,X\right]\\
= \;&\int_{\R}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{A}}K(v)\!\left[p_A\!\left(\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))+\delta_n v\right) - p_A\!\left(\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))+\delta_n v\right)\right]\mathrm{d}v\right\}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon.
\end{align*}
By the mean-value theorem, $|p_A(\theta+\delta_n v) - p_A(\theta'+\delta_n v)|\leq \sup_a|\partial_a p_A(a)|\cdot|\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))-\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))|$.  Therefore,
\begin{align*}
&\left|\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))) - K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))\bigg|U,X\right]\right|\\
\leq\;& \sup_a|\partial_a p_A(a)|\cdot\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty \int_{\R}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{A}}K(v)\,\mathrm{d}v\right\}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon = C'\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty,
\end{align*}
where $C' := \sup_a|\partial_a p_A(a)|$ is finite by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bound on Conditional Kernel Integral Expectation]\label{lemma:kernel_int_exp_bound}
Let $\theta,\theta'\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$.  Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and \ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, there exists a constant $C'>0$ such that
$$\E\!\left[\int_{\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\;\Bigg|\;U,X\right] \leq C'\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.$$
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\noindent\textit{Step 1: Conditional independence of $A_i$ and the integration limits.}

The integration limits $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ and $\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ are stochastic: from \eqref{eq:widehat_U_i}, $\widehat{U}(S_i)$ depends on the outcome noise $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$, while $A_i = g(X_i,U(S_i))+\xi_i$ depends on the treatment noise $\xi_i$.  Since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ conditional on $(U,X)$ by the data-generating model \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}, the integration limits and $A_i$ are \emph{conditionally independent} given $(U,X)$.

\noindent\textit{Step 2: Rewrite the integral as an indicator and apply Fubini.}

Writing the integral as an indicator function over $a$ and applying Fubini's theorem to swap the integral over $a$ with the expectation over $A_i$ and $\widehat{U}(S_i)$:
\begin{align*}
&\E\!\left[\int_{\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\;\Bigg|\;U,X\right]\\
=\;&\int_{\R}\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\cdot\mathbf{1}\!\left(\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\leq a\leq\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\Big|\,U,X\right]\mathrm{d}a\\
=\;&\int_{\R}\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\cdot\E\!\left[\mathbf{1}\!\left(\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\leq a\leq\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\Big|\,U,X\right]\mathrm{d}a,
\end{align*}
where the last equality uses the conditional independence of $A_i$ and the limits given $(U,X)$.

\noindent\textit{Step 3: Bound the two conditional expectations separately.}

For the kernel factor, by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} the conditional density $p^*(A_i|X_i,U(S_i))$ is bounded above by $C'$, and $K_{\delta_n}$ integrates to one:
$$\sup_{a,u,x}\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\big|\,U(S_i)=u,X_i=x\right] = \sup_{a,u,x}\int K_{\delta_n}(t-a)\,p^*(t|x,u)\,\mathrm{d}t \leq C'\int K_{\delta_n}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t = C'.$$
Crucially, the $\delta_n^{-1}$ factor inside $K_{\delta_n}$ is absorbed by the width-$\delta_n$ support of the kernel, leaving an $O(1)$ bound independent of $\delta_n$.

For the indicator factor, interchanging the integral over $a$ with the outer expectation:
\begin{align*}
&\int_{\R}\E\!\left[\mathbf{1}\!\left(\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))
\leq a\leq\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\Big|\,U,X\right]\mathrm{d}a\\
= &\E\!\left[|\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))-\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))|\,\big|\,U,X\right]
\leq \|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Step 4: Combine.}

Multiplying the two bounds and collecting constants,
\begin{align}
\E\!\left[\int_{\theta'(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\;\Bigg|\;U,X\right]
\leq C'\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty.\label{eq:kernel_int_diff_exp}
\end{align}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bound on Integrated Kernel Expectation from $a_L$]\label{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound}
Let $\theta:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{A}$ be a measurable function with $\theta(x,u)\in\mathcal{A}=[a_L,a_H]$ for all $(x,u)$.  Under Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, there exists a constant $C>0$ (depending only on $\sup_a p_A(a)$ and $|\mathcal{A}|$) such that
$$\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))} K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\bigg|\,U,X\right] \leq C.$$
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\noindent\textit{Step 1: Indicator reformulation and Fubini.}

Write the integration range as an indicator and apply Fubini's theorem to exchange the integral over $a$ with the expectation:
\begin{align*}
&\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))} K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\bigg|\,U,X\right]\\
=\;&\int_{\R}\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\cdot\mathbf{1}\!\left(a_L\leq a\leq \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\Big|\,U,X\right]\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Step 2: Factor using conditional independence.}

By the data-generating model \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}, $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$ while $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ through $\widehat{U}(S_i)$.  Since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ conditionally on $(U,X)$, the indicator $\mathbf{1}(a_L\leq a\leq \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))$ and $K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)$ are conditionally independent given $(U,X)$.  Hence the expectation factors:
\begin{align*}
=\;&\int_{\R}\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\cdot\E\!\left[\mathbf{1}\!\left(a_L\leq a\leq \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\Big|\,U,X\right]\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textit{Step 3: Bound each factor.}

\emph{Kernel factor.} For fixed $a\in\mathcal{A}$, integrating over the distribution of $A_i$ and substituting $v=(a'-a)/\delta_n$,
$$\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\big|\,U,X\right] = \int_{\R}K(v)\,p_A(a+\delta_n v)\,\mathrm{d}v \leq \sup_{a'}p_A(a')\int_{\R}K(v)\,\mathrm{d}v = C' < \infty,$$
uniformly in $a$ and $\delta_n$, by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}.

\emph{Indicator factor.} Exchanging the integral over $a$ with the conditional expectation via Fubini,
\begin{align*}
\int_{\R}\E\!\left[\mathbf{1}\!\left(a_L\leq a\leq \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\Big|\,U,X\right]\mathrm{d}a
= \E\!\left[\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))-a_L\,\big|\,U,X\right]
\leq a_H - a_L =: |\mathcal{A}|,
\end{align*}
since $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\in[a_L,a_H]$ by assumption.

\noindent\textit{Step 4: Combine.}

Multiplying the two bounds,
$$\E\!\left[\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))} K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\,\bigg|\,U,X\right] \leq C'\cdot|\mathcal{A}| =: C.$$
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bound on Conditional Kernel Expectation]\label{lemma:kernel_theta_exp_bound}
Let $\theta:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{A}$ be a measurable function.  Under Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, there exists a constant $C>0$ such that
$$\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i - \theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\bigg|U,X\right] \leq C.$$
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The argument is identical to that of Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_exp_bound}.  By the data-generating model \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}, $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$ while $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$; since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ given $(U,X)$, the two are conditionally independent given $(U,X)$.  Let $p_{\tilde\varepsilon}$ be the density of $\tilde\varepsilon_i=\sum_{j\neq i}\alpha_{ij}\varepsilon_j$ given $(U,X)$.  Applying Fubini and the substitution $v=(a-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i)))/\delta_n$,
\begin{align*}
\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}\!\left(A_i-\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))\right)\bigg|U,X\right]
&= \int_{\R}\!\left\{\int_{\mathcal{A}}K(v)\,p_A\!\left(\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))+\delta_n v\right)\mathrm{d}v\right\}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon\\
&\leq \sup_{a}p_A(a)\cdot\underbrace{\int_{\R}K(v)\,\mathrm{d}v}_{=1}\cdot\underbrace{\int_{\R}p_{\tilde\varepsilon}(\tilde\varepsilon)\,\mathrm{d}\tilde\varepsilon}_{=1}
= \sup_a p_A(a) =: C,
\end{align*}
where $C<\infty$ by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{Auxiliary Lemmas for Sub-Gaussianity: Absolute Bound}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_bound}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}.  Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, the loss is uniformly bounded:
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p)\right| \leq C.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We bound each of the two terms in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} separately.

\noindent\textbf{First term.}  The integration interval $[a_L,\theta(x,u)]\subseteq[a_L,a_H]$ has length at most $a_H-a_L=|\mathcal{A}|$.  By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, the integrand satisfies $|\mathcal{T}-f(a,x)-u|\leq C^\prime$ uniformly.  Therefore,
\begin{align*}
\left|\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}(\mathcal{T}-f(a,x)-u)\,\mathrm{d}a\right|
\leq |\mathcal{A}|\cdot C^\prime.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Second term.}  By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|y-f(a,x)-u|\leq C^\prime$.  By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p(a|x,u)\geq c^\prime>0$.  The integrated kernel satisfies $\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime\leq\int_\R K_{\delta_n}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=1$.  Therefore,
\begin{align*}
\left|\frac{y-f(a,x)-u}{p(a|x,u)}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(x,u)}K_{\delta_n}(a-a^\prime)\,\mathrm{d}a^\prime\right|
\leq \frac{C^\prime}{c^\prime}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Combining.}  Adding the two bounds and setting $C:=|\mathcal{A}|C^\prime+C^\prime/c^\prime$,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{y,a,u,x}\left|L^{(\delta_n)}((y,a,u,x);\theta,\psi,f,p)\right|\leq C.
\end{align*}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_U}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}.  Let $U=(U(S_1),\ldots,U(S_n))$ and $X=(X_1,\ldots,X_n)$ be as in \eqref{eq:data_generative_model_1}--\eqref{eq:data_generative_model_2}.  Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, for fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$, $p\in\mathcal{F}_p$, the conditional expectation of the $\widehat{U}(S_i)$-derivative of the loss satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)}\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
        \leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $u=\widehat U(S_i)$ for brevity.  Applying the Leibniz rule to $L^{(\delta_n)}_i$ in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} with respect to $u$ yields five terms:
\begin{align*}
\partial_u L^{(\delta_n)}_i
=\;&\underbrace{(\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(X_i,u),X_i)-u)\,\partial_u\theta(X_i,u)}_{\mathrm{Term\ (I)}}
  - \underbrace{(\theta(X_i,u)-a_L)}_{\mathrm{Term\ (II)}}\\
&+\underbrace{\frac{Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\,K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(X_i,u))\,\partial_u\theta(X_i,u)}_{\mathrm{Term\ (III)}}\\
&-\underbrace{\frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,u)}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (IV)}}\\
&-\underbrace{\frac{(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u)\,\partial_u p(A_i|X_i,u)}{p(A_i|X_i,u)^2}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,u)}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a}_{\mathrm{Term\ (V)}},
\end{align*}
where Terms (I)--(II) arise from differentiating the first integral in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i} via the boundary and integrand, respectively, and Terms (III)--(V) arise from differentiating the IPW term via the upper limit of $\int K$, the residual numerator, and the propensity score denominator, respectively.

\noindent\textbf{Conditional expectation and independence.}
Given $(U,X)$, $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ depends on $\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ while $A_i$ depends on $\xi_i$; since $\xi_i\perp\{\varepsilon_j\}_{j\neq i}$ given $(U,X)$, expectation of products of functions of $A_i$ and $\theta(X_i,\widehat{U}(S_i))$ factor conditionally on $(U,X)$.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (I).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|\mathcal{T}-f(\theta(X_i,u),X_i)-u|\leq C^\prime$.  By Lemmas~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}, $|\partial_u\theta(X_i,u)|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$.  Thus,
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (I)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right| \leq C^\prime\cdot C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (II).}
Since $\theta(X_i,u)\in[a_L,a_H]$, we have $|\theta(X_i,u)-a_L|\leq |\mathcal{A}|$.  This constant is absorbed into $C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$ in the relevant rate regime.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (III).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-u|\leq C^\prime$.  By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p\geq c^\prime>0$.  By Lemmas~\ref{lemma:derivative_svm} and~\ref{lemma:bound_svm}, $|\partial_u\theta|\leq C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$.  Taking the conditional expectation and using Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_theta_exp_bound},
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (III)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq \frac{C^\prime}{c^\prime}\cdot C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\cdot\E\!\left[K_{\delta_n}(A_i-\theta(X_i,u))\,\big|\,U,X\right]
\leq \frac{C^\prime C^2}{c^\prime}\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (IV).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $1/p\leq 1/c^\prime$.  By Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound},
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (IV)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq \frac{1}{c^\prime}\cdot C.
\end{align*}
This constant is absorbed into $C\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}$ in the relevant rate regime.

\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term (V).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, $|Y_i-f-u|\leq C^\prime$ and $|\partial_u p|\leq C^\prime$.  By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, $p\geq c^\prime$.  By Lemma~\ref{lemma:kernel_int_theta_bound},
\begin{align*}
\left|\E\!\left[\mathrm{Term\ (V)}\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq \frac{C^{\prime 2}}{c^{\prime 2}}\cdot C.
\end{align*}
This constant is similarly absorbed.

\noindent\textbf{Final assembly.}
Collecting the five bounds and noting that $\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\geq 1$ in the relevant rate regime so that Terms (II), (IV), (V) are dominated by Terms (I) and (III),
$$\left|\partial_{\widehat U(S_i)}\E\!\left[L^{(\delta_n)}_i(\theta;\psi,f,p)\,\big|\,U,X\right]\right|
\leq C\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}.$$
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lemma:L_i_derivative_Y}
    Let $L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)$ be the loss defined in \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}.  Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, for fixed $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$, $p\in\mathcal{F}_p$:
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i}\left|\partial_{Y_i}L^{(\delta_n)}((Y_i,A_i,\widehat U(S_i),X_i);\theta,\psi,f,p)\right| \leq \frac{1}{c^\prime}.
    \end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
From \eqref{eq:loss_function_i}, $Y_i$ appears only in the IPW term through the residual $(Y_i-f(A_i,X_i)-\widehat U(S_i))$ in the numerator; the first integral does not involve $Y_i$.  Differentiating with respect to $Y_i$,
\begin{align*}
\partial_{Y_i}L^{(\delta_n)}_i
= \frac{1}{p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}\int_{a_L}^{\theta(X_i,\widehat U(S_i))}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a.
\end{align*}
Bounding each factor: $1/p(A_i|X_i,\widehat U(S_i))\leq 1/c^\prime$ by Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap}, and $\int_{a_L}^{\theta}K_{\delta_n}(A_i-a)\,\mathrm{d}a\leq\int_\R K_{\delta_n}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=1$.  Therefore,
\begin{align*}
\left|\partial_{Y_i}L^{(\delta_n)}_i\right| \leq \frac{1}{c^\prime}.
\end{align*}
Taking the supremum over $(Y_i,A_i,\widehat{U}(S_i),X_i)$ yields the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Weak Convergence Rate}
In Section \ref{sec:sub-gaussianity}, we derived the sub-Gaussianity of $R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)-\E[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)]$ w.r.t. $\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}, f\in\mathcal{F}_f, p\in\mathcal{F}_p, \psi\in\Psi$. In this section, we combine them with the complexity of the parameter space, i.e., rate of covering number to derive the weak convergence rate: 
$$\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}, f\in\mathcal{F}_f, p\in\mathcal{F}_p, \psi\in\Psi}R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)-\E[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)].$$ 
\subsubsection{Revisit Sub-Gaussianity}
For $\theta,\theta^\prime\in\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$, we have  
\begin{align}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta^\prime;\psi,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta^\prime;\psi,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\nonumber\\
    \leq & C\log(n) n^{-1/2}\delta_n^{-1}\|\theta - \theta^\prime\|_{\infty} + C\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}n^{-1/2}\log(n)\cdot\left( \gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/4}\sqrt{\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty} + \gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\|\theta-\theta'\|_\infty\right) \nonumber\\
    = & B^1_\theta(n)\|\theta - \theta^\prime\| + B^2_\theta(n)\|\theta - \theta^\prime\|^{1/2}\label{eq:sub_gaussian_theta}
\end{align}
For $ f,f^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_f$, we have 
\begin{align}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f^\prime,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\nonumber\\
    \leq & C\left(1+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\right)\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|f-f^\prime\|_\infty\nonumber\\
    = & B_f(n) \|f-f^\prime\|_\infty. \label{eq:sub_gaussian_f}
\end{align}
For $ p,p^\prime\in\mathcal{F}_p$, we have 
\begin{align}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi,f,p^\prime)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\nonumber\\
    \leq & C\left(1+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\right)\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty\nonumber\\
    = & B_p(n)\|p-p^\prime\|_\infty. \label{eq:sub_gaussian_p}
\end{align}
For $\psi,\psi^\prime\in\Psi$, we have 
\begin{align}
&\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-R_n^{({\delta_n})}(\theta;\psi^\prime,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\nonumber\\
    \leq & C\left(\delta_n^{-1}+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}(1+\gamma_n^{-1})\right)\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\log(n)^{3/2}\,n^{-1/2}\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\nonumber\\
    = & B_\psi(n)\left\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi^\prime)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}. \label{eq:sub_gaussian_psi} 
\end{align}
Also, for the empirical loss, we have 
\begin{align}
    &\left\|R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)-\E\left[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\psi,f,p)\right]\right\|_{\psi_2}\nonumber\\
    \leq &C\left(1+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\right)\log(n)\,n^{-1/2}\nonumber\\
    =& B_L(n).\label{eq:sub_gaussian_L}
\end{align}

\subsubsection{Covering Number of Parameter Space}

\noindent By Assumption \ref{condition:excess_risk:VC-type}, $\mathcal{F}_f$ and $\mathcal{F}_p$ satisfy the Donsker condition with respect to $\|\cdot\|_{P,2}$ norm. We show below that under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and~\ref{condition:excess_risk:VC-type}, $\mathcal{F}_f$ and $\mathcal{F}_p$ also satisfy the Donsker condition with respect to the supremum norm $\|\cdot\|_\infty$. The key ingredient is a Lipschitz interpolation inequality that converts the VC-type (logarithmic) entropy in $\|\cdot\|_{L_2(P)}$ into logarithmic entropy in $\|\cdot\|_\infty$. Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:overlap} is not required for this result.

\begin{lemma}[Donsker Condition under $L^\infty$]\label{lemma:covering_number_Linfty}
Under Assumptions~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth} and~\ref{condition:excess_risk:VC-type}, the function classes $\mathcal{F}_f$ and $\mathcal{F}_p$ satisfy the Donsker condition with respect to $\|\cdot\|_\infty$. Specifically, for every $\epsilon > 0$,
\begin{align*}
\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\,\epsilon)
&\;\leq\; \frac{V(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}{2}\,\log\!\frac{C'\!D}{\epsilon},\\
\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_p,\,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\,\epsilon)
&\;\leq\; \frac{V(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}{2}\,\log\!\frac{C'\!D}{\epsilon},
\end{align*}
where $V$ and $A$ are the VC-type constants in Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:VC-type}, $D$ is the diameter of $\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{U}$, $d_{\mathcal{D}}=\dim\mathcal{D}$, and $C$ absorbs universal constants. In particular,
\[
\int_0^{D}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon \;<\;\infty,
\]
and the same holds for $\mathcal{F}_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We argue for $\mathcal{F}_f$; the proof for $\mathcal{F}_p$ is identical. The proof combines a Lipschitz interpolation inequality with the VC-type covering number bound.

\noindent\textbf{Step 1 (Lipschitz interpolation).}
By Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:smooth}, every $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$ is $C'$-Lipschitz on $\mathcal{D}$ (since $\|\nabla f\|_\infty\leq C'$). Consequently, for any $h = f-g$ with $f,g\in\mathcal{F}_f$, $h$ is $2C'$-Lipschitz. We claim:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lipschitz_interpolation}
\|h\|_\infty \;\leq\; C\,(2C')^{d_{\mathcal{D}}/(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}\,\|h\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})}^{2/(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}.
\end{equation}
To see this, fix any $x\in\mathcal{D}$ and radius $r>0$. By Lipschitz continuity, $|h(x)-h(y)|\leq 2C' r$ for all $y\in B(x,r)\cap\mathcal{D}$. Averaging over $B(x,r)\cap\mathcal{D}$ and applying the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality:
\[
|h(x)| \;\leq\; 2C' r + \frac{\|h\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})}}{\sqrt{|B(x,r)\cap\mathcal{D}|}}
\;\leq\; 2C' r + \frac{C\,\|h\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})}}{r^{d_{\mathcal{D}}/2}}.
\]
Minimising the right-hand side over $r>0$ by setting $r^* = \bigl(C\|h\|_{L^2}/(2C')\bigr)^{2/(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}$ yields \eqref{eq:lipschitz_interpolation}.

\noindent\textbf{Step 2 (Converting $L^2$ covers to $L^\infty$ covers).}
Since Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:VC-type} gives the VC-type bound for \emph{all} probability measures $Q$ — in particular for normalised Lebesgue measure $\mathrm{Leb}$ on $\mathcal{D}$ — we have:
\[
\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})},\epsilon_0) \;\leq\; V\log\frac{A}{\epsilon_0}.
\]
Now let $\{f_1,\ldots,f_N\}$ be a minimal $\epsilon_0$-cover of $\mathcal{F}_f$ in $\|\cdot\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})}$. For any $f\in\mathcal{F}_f$, pick $f_j$ with $\|f-f_j\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})}\leq\epsilon_0$. By \eqref{eq:lipschitz_interpolation}:
\[
\|f-f_j\|_\infty \;\leq\; C\,(2C')^{d_{\mathcal{D}}/(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}\,\epsilon_0^{2/(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}.
\]
Hence the same set $\{f_1,\ldots,f_N\}$ is an $\epsilon$-cover in $\|\cdot\|_\infty$ with $\epsilon = C(2C')^{d/(d+2)}\epsilon_0^{2/(d+2)}$. Inverting: to achieve an $\epsilon$-cover in $\|\cdot\|_\infty$, it suffices to take $\epsilon_0 = (\epsilon/(C(2C')^{d/(d+2)}))^{(d+2)/2}$. Therefore:
\[
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon) \;\leq\; \mathcal{N}\!\left(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_{L^2(\mathrm{Leb})},\left(\frac{\epsilon}{C(2C')^{d/(d+2)}}\right)^{(d+2)/2}\right).
\]

\noindent\textbf{Step 3 (Logarithmic entropy bound in $L^\infty$).}
Substituting the VC-type bound:
\begin{align*}
\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)
&\;\leq\; V\log\frac{A}{(\epsilon/(C(2C')^{d/(d+2)}))^{(d+2)/2}}\\
&\;=\; V\cdot\frac{d_{\mathcal{D}}+2}{2}\cdot\log\frac{C'D}{\epsilon},
\end{align*}
where all constants $(A, C, 2C')$ have been absorbed into the diameter $D$ and constant prefactors.

\noindent\textbf{Step 4 (Entropy integral).}
\[
\int_0^D\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;\leq\; \sqrt{\frac{V(d_{\mathcal{D}}+2)}{2}}\int_0^D\sqrt{\log\frac{C'D}{\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon.
\]
Substituting $\epsilon = D\,e^{-t}$ gives $\mathrm{d}\epsilon = D e^{-t}\,\mathrm{d}t$ and $\log(C'D/\epsilon) = \log C' + t$, so the integral becomes $D\int_0^\infty\sqrt{\log C'+t}\,e^{-t}\,\mathrm{d}t \leq D\int_0^\infty(\sqrt{\log C'}+\sqrt{t})\,e^{-t}\,\mathrm{d}t = D(\sqrt{\log C'} + \Gamma(3/2)) < \infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The Lipschitz structure is essential: without it, converting $L^2(P)$ Donsker to $L^\infty$ Donsker may fail. For instance, a class with $\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L^2},\epsilon)\leq C\epsilon^{-2\alpha}$ (Donsker for $\alpha<1$) would yield $\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_\infty,\eta)\leq C\eta^{-\alpha(d+2)}$, whose entropy integral diverges for $\alpha(d+2)\geq 2$. The VC-type (logarithmic) structure in Assumption~\ref{condition:excess_risk:VC-type} is what keeps the $L^\infty$ entropy logarithmic after the norm conversion.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Donsker Condition for GP Covariance Parameter Space]\label{lemma:covering_number_psi}
Suppose the covariance parameter space $\Psi\subset\mathbb{R}^p$ is compact with Euclidean diameter $D_\Psi$, and the covariance function $C(\cdot,\cdot;\psi)$ is continuously differentiable in $\psi\in\Psi$ with uniformly bounded gradient:
\[
\kappa \;:=\; \sup_{s,s'\in\mathcal{S},\;\psi\in\Psi}\|\nabla_\psi C(s,s';\psi)\|_{\mathbb{R}^p} \;<\; \infty.
\]
Equip $\Psi$ with the semi-metric $\|\psi-\psi'\|_\Psi := \|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi')\|_{\mathrm{op}}$, where $\Sigma_n(\psi)$ is the $n\times n$ covariance matrix with $(i,j)$-entry $C(S_i,S_j;\psi)$. Then the covering numbers satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:covering_psi}
\log\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,\epsilon) \;\leq\; p\log\!\frac{Cn\kappa D_\Psi}{\epsilon}
\end{equation}
for a universal constant $C$, and $\Psi$ satisfies the Donsker condition:
\[
\int_0^{D}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon \;<\;\infty,
\]
where $D:=\sup_{\psi,\psi'\in\Psi}\|\psi-\psi'\|_\Psi\leq n\kappa D_\Psi$ is the diameter of $\Psi$ under $\|\cdot\|_\Psi$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\noindent\textbf{Step 1 (Per-entry Lipschitz bound).}
Fix any $\psi,\psi'\in\Psi$ and any pair of locations $S_i,S_j$. By the mean value theorem applied to $\psi\mapsto C(S_i,S_j;\psi)$ along the segment from $\psi'$ to $\psi$,
\[
\bigl|C(S_i,S_j;\psi)-C(S_i,S_j;\psi')\bigr|
\;\leq\; \|\nabla_\psi C(S_i,S_j;\bar\psi)\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}\,\|\psi-\psi'\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}
\;\leq\; \kappa\|\psi-\psi'\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}
\]
for some $\bar\psi$ on the segment.

\noindent\textbf{Step 2 (Spectral norm Lipschitz bound).}
Let $\Delta_n(\psi,\psi'):=\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi')$, whose $(i,j)$-entry satisfies $|\Delta_{ij}|\leq\kappa\|\psi-\psi'\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}$ by Step~1. For the spectral norm of a symmetric matrix, $\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|A\|_{\infty} = \max_i\sum_j|a_{ij}|$. Since $\Delta_n$ has $n$ columns and each entry is bounded by $\kappa\|\psi-\psi'\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}$:
\[
\|\Sigma_n(\psi)-\Sigma_n(\psi')\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\;\leq\; \max_i\sum_{j=1}^n|\Delta_{ij}|
\;\leq\; n\kappa\|\psi-\psi'\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}.
\]
Hence the identity map $(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{R}^p})\to(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi)$ is $(n\kappa)$-Lipschitz.

\noindent\textbf{Step 3 (Covering number bound).}
Any $\delta$-cover of $\Psi$ in $\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{R}^p}$ is an $(n\kappa\delta)$-cover in $\|\cdot\|_\Psi$. Therefore, setting $\delta=\epsilon/(n\kappa)$:
\[
\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,\epsilon)
\;\leq\; \mathcal{N}\!\left(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{R}^p},\frac{\epsilon}{n\kappa}\right).
\]
Since $\Psi\subset\mathbb{R}^p$ is compact with Euclidean diameter $D_\Psi$, a standard volumetric argument yields
\[
\mathcal{N}\!\left(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{R}^p},\frac{\epsilon}{n\kappa}\right)
\;\leq\; \left(\frac{2n\kappa D_\Psi}{\epsilon}+1\right)^p
\;\leq\; \left(\frac{Cn\kappa D_\Psi}{\epsilon}\right)^p
\]
for $\epsilon\leq n\kappa D_\Psi$, giving \eqref{eq:covering_psi}: $\log\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,\epsilon)\leq p\log(Cn\kappa D_\Psi/\epsilon)$.

\noindent\textbf{Step 4 (Entropy integral).}
With $D=n\kappa D_\Psi$ (an upper bound on the diameter of $\Psi$ under $\|\cdot\|_\Psi$):
\[
\int_0^D\!\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;\leq\; \sqrt{p}\int_0^D\!\sqrt{\log\!\tfrac{D}{\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon.
\]
The substitution $\epsilon=De^{-t}$ gives $\mathrm{d}\epsilon = De^{-t}\mathrm{d}t$ and $\log(D/\epsilon)=t$, so
\[
\int_0^D\sqrt{\log\!\tfrac{D}{\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;=\; D\int_0^\infty\sqrt{t}\,e^{-t}\,\mathrm{d}t
\;=\; D\,\Gamma\!\left(\tfrac{3}{2}\right)
\;=\; \frac{D\sqrt{\pi}}{2}
\;<\;\infty,
\]
completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The gradient bound $\kappa<\infty$ holds for any covariance function that is $C^1$ in $\psi$ on a compact set $\Psi$. In particular, it holds for the Mat\'{e}rn covariance
\[
C_{\mathrm{Mat}}(s,s';\sigma^2,\ell,\nu)
= \frac{\sigma^2 2^{1-\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)}
\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{2\nu}\|s-s'\|}{\ell}\right)^{\!\nu}\!
K_\nu\!\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{2\nu}\|s-s'\|}{\ell}\right),
\]
whenever $\Psi$ is bounded away from $0$ in all coordinates $(\sigma^2,\ell,\nu)$, since the partial derivatives w.r.t.\ $(\sigma^2,\ell,\nu)$ are continuous and bounded on such sets.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Covering Number of Gaussian RKHS Ball]\label{lemma:covering_number_rkhs}
Let $\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}$ be the RKHS of the Gaussian kernel $K_{\gamma_n}$ on a compact domain $\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^d$, and let $B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2})=\{\theta\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}:\|\theta\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}\leq\lambda_n^{-1/2}\}$ be the RKHS ball of radius $\lambda_n^{-1/2}$. For any $p\in(0,1)$, there exists a constant $C_p<\infty$ (depending on $p$, $d$, and the domain but not on $n$, $\gamma_n$, or $\lambda_n$) such that
\[
\ln\mathcal{N}\!\left(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\,\epsilon_\theta\right)
\;\leq\; C_p\,\gamma_n^{-d}\,\lambda_n^{-p}\,\epsilon_\theta^{-2p}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof combines Theorem~\ref{thm:entropy_gaussian} and Theorem~\ref{thm:equivalence_covering_entropy} in three steps.

\noindent\textbf{Step 1: Entropy numbers of the unit RKHS ball.}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:entropy_gaussian}, for any integer $m\geq 1$ and all $i\geq 1$,
\[
e_i\!\left(\mathrm{id}:H_{\gamma_n}(rX)\to\ell_\infty(rX)\right)
\;\leq\; c_{m,d}(X)\,r^m\,\gamma_n^{-m}\,i^{-m/d},
\]
i.e., the unit RKHS ball $B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(1)$ can be covered by $2^{i-1}$ sup-norm balls of radius $c_{m,d}(X)r^m\gamma_n^{-m}i^{-m/d}$.

\noindent\textbf{Step 2: Scale to radius $\lambda_n^{-1/2}$.}
Since entropy numbers scale linearly with the ball radius,
\[
e_i\!\left(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\|\cdot\|_\infty\right)
= \lambda_n^{-1/2}\cdot e_i\!\left(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(1),\|\cdot\|_\infty\right)
\;\leq\; \underbrace{c_{m,d}(X)\,r^m\,\gamma_n^{-m}\,\lambda_n^{-1/2}}_{=:\,a}\cdot i^{-m/d}.
\]

\noindent\textbf{Step 3: Convert entropy numbers to covering numbers.}
The bound in Step~2 has the form $e_i\leq a\cdot i^{-1/q}$ with $q=d/m$. Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:equivalence_covering_entropy},
\begin{align*}
\ln\mathcal{N}\!\left(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon_\theta\right)
&\leq \ln(4)\left(\frac{a}{\epsilon_\theta}\right)^{d/m}\\
&= \ln(4)\cdot c_{m,d}(X)^{d/m}\cdot r^d\cdot\gamma_n^{-d}\cdot\lambda_n^{-d/(2m)}\cdot\epsilon_\theta^{-d/m}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Reparametrize via $p=d/(2m)$ and absorb.}
Given any target $p\in(0,1)$, choose the integer $m=\lceil d/(2p)\rceil$. Since $p\leq 1$ implies $m\geq d/2$, and since $m\geq d/(2p)$ gives $d/(2m)\leq p$ and $d/m\leq 2p$, we have (for $\lambda_n,\epsilon_\theta\leq 1$):
\[
\lambda_n^{-d/(2m)}\leq\lambda_n^{-p}, \qquad \epsilon_\theta^{-d/m}\leq\epsilon_\theta^{-2p}.
\]
Setting $C_p:=\ln(4)\cdot c_{m,d}(X)^{d/m}\cdot r^d$ (finite for each fixed $p$, $d$, and domain) yields
\[
\ln\mathcal{N}\!\left(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon_\theta\right)
\;\leq\; C_p\,\gamma_n^{-d}\,\lambda_n^{-p}\,\epsilon_\theta^{-2p},
\]
as claimed.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Union Bound \& Chaining}


Let $D_\theta$ be the diameter of of $\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{K}}$. By Lemma \ref{lemma:bound_svm}, $D_\theta = O(\lambda_n^{-1/2})$. Let $\mathcal{C}^k_\theta$ be the $\epsilon^k_\theta$-net of $\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{K}}$ where $\epsilon^k_\theta = D_\theta 2^{-k}$. We let $\pi^k(\theta)$ be the element in $\mathcal{C}^k_\theta$ that is closest to $\theta$ in $\mathcal{H}_\mathcal{K}$. We also define parallel notations for parameter class $\mathcal{F}_f,\mathcal{F}_p$ and $\Psi$. Denote $\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)$ as the centralized empirical loss:
$$\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi):=R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)-\E[R_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)].$$
Then, we do the chaining decomposition as follows:
 The chaining decomposition of $\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)$ is:
\begin{align*}
   \bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi) &= \underbrace{\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi_0(\theta);\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))}_{\text{base term}}\\
   & +\sum_{k=0}^\infty\underbrace{\left\{\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi_{k+1}(\theta);\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k+1}(p),\pi_{k+1}(\psi)) - \bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi_{k}(\theta);\pi_{k}(f),\pi_{k}(p),\pi_{k}(\psi))\right\}}_{\text{$k$-th increment: }\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)}. 
\end{align*}
By the sub-Gaussianity of the empirical risk in equation \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_L}, we have the following bound for base term with probability no less than $1-2e^{-\tau}$,
\begin{align*}
    \left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))\right|\leq CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau}
\end{align*}
By the sub-Gaussianity of the empirical risk w.r.t. $\theta,f,p$ and $\psi$ in Equation \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_theta}, \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_f}, \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_p}, \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_psi}, the $k$-th increment satisfies:
$$\|\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)\|_{\psi_2}\leq \sigma_k = \sigma_0^{(1)}2^{-k} + \sigma_0^{(2)}2^{-k/2}.$$
Here, $\sigma_k$ is defined as:
\begin{align*}
    \sigma_k:= & C2^{-k}\{B_p(n) D_{p} + B_f(n)D_{f}+ B_\psi(n)D_{\psi}+ B_\theta^{(1)}(n)D_\theta\} + CB_\theta^{(2)}(n)\sqrt{2^{-k}D_\theta}\\
    =:& \sigma_0^{(1)}2^{-k} + \sigma_0^{(2)}2^{-k/2}
\end{align*}
The number of distinct values of $(\pi_{k}(\theta),\pi_{k}(\psi),\pi_{k}(f),\pi_{k}(p))$ is $N_{k} = |\mathcal{C}^{k}_\theta||\mathcal{C}^{k}_\psi||\mathcal{C}^{k}_f||\mathcal{C}^{k}_p|$. The number of distinct values of $(\pi_{k+1}(\theta),\pi_{k+1}(\psi),\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k+1}(p))$ is $N_{k+1} = |\mathcal{C}^{k+1}_\theta||\mathcal{C}^{k+1}_\psi||\mathcal{C}^{k+1}_f||\mathcal{C}^{k+1}_p|$. Also, we have $N_{k+1}\geq N_k$. By the union bound, for any $\tau_k>0$, 
\begin{align*}
\pr\left(\sup_{\theta,\psi,f,p}|\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)|\geq t\right)
    \leq & N_{k+1}N_k\pr\left(|\Delta_k|\geq t\right)\\
    \leq & N_{k+1}^2\pr\left(|\Delta_k|\geq t\right)\\
    \leq & \exp\left(-Ct^2/\sigma_k^2+2\log(N_{k+1})\right)
\end{align*}
Thus, for any $\tau_k>0$, with probability no less than $1-2e^{-\tau_k}$, 
$$\sup_{\theta,\psi,f,p}|\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)|\leq C\sigma_k\sqrt{\tau_k +2\log(N_{k+1}))}.$$
Combine the high probability bound for base term and $k$-th increment and let $\tau_k = \tau+k$, we have that with probability no less than $1-2e^{-\tau}(1+\sum_{k=1}^\infty e^{-k})\geq 1- 6e^{-\tau}$, 
\begin{align*}
    & \sup_{\theta,\psi,f,p}|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)|\\
    \leq & \left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi_0(\theta);\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))\right|\\
    & +\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sup_{\theta,\psi,f,p}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi_{k+1}(\theta);\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k+1}(p),\pi_{k+1}(\psi)) - \bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi_{k}(\theta);\pi_{k}(f),\pi_{k}(p),\pi_{k}(\psi))\right|\\
    \leq & CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau} + C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{\tau + k +2\log(N_{k+1}))}\\
    \leq & CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau}+C\underbrace{\sqrt{\tau}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k}_{\text{Term 1}} + C\underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{k}}_{\text{Term 2}} + C\underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{2\log(N_{k+1})}}_{\text{Term 3}}
\end{align*}
\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term 1: }Substitute $\sigma_k$ into the first sum. This splits into two standard geometric series:$$C\sqrt{\tau}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k = C\sqrt{\tau} \left( \sigma_0^{(1)} \sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k} + \sigma_0^{(2)} \sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2} \right).$$
Because $\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k} = 2$ and $\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2} = \frac{1}{1 - 1/\sqrt{2}} = 2 + \sqrt{2}$, they can be absorbed into $C$:
$$C\sqrt{\tau}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k \leq C \sqrt{\tau} \left( \sigma_0^{(1)} + \sigma_0^{(2)} \right).$$
\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term 2: }Substitute $\sigma_k$ into the second sum. This splits into:$$C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{k} = C \left( \sigma_0^{(1)} \sum_{k=0}^\infty \sqrt{k} 2^{-k} + \sigma_0^{(2)} \sum_{k=0}^\infty \sqrt{k} 2^{-k/2} \right)$$
Because $\sum_{k=0}^\infty \sqrt{k} 2^{-k}$ and $ \sum_{k=0}^\infty \sqrt{k} 2^{-k/2}$ are finite, they can be absorbed into $C$:
$$C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{k} \leq C_2 \left( \sigma_0^{(1)} + \sigma_0^{(2)} \right).$$
\noindent\textbf{Bounding Term 3: }Substitute $\sigma_k$ into the third sum, where $N_k$ is the covering number at scale $\epsilon_k = 2^{-k}$:
$$C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{2\log(N_k)} = C\sqrt{2} \left( \sigma_0^{(1)} \sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}} + \sigma_0^{(2)} \sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}} \right).$$
Since $N_{k+1}$ is the product of the cardinality of each $\epsilon$-net, i.e.,  the covering number of the corresponding parameter space, then, wusing the subadditivity of the square root, ($\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^4 a_j}\leq\sum_{j=1}^4\sqrt{a_j}$ for $a_j\geq 0$), we have
\begin{align*}
&\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}}
\;\\
\leq\;&\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,D_f2^{-(k+1)})}
+\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_p,\|\cdot\|_\infty,D_p2^{-(k+1)})}\\
&+\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,D_\psi2^{-(k+1)})}
+\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\|\cdot\|_\infty,D_\theta2^{-(k+1)})}.
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Step 3a: Riemann-sum-to-Dudley-integral.}
For any function class $\mathcal{F}$ with diameter $D$ under the relevant norm, the covering-number map $\epsilon\mapsto\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon)}$ is decreasing. Setting $\epsilon_k := D\cdot 2^{-k}$, the interval widths are $\epsilon_k - \epsilon_{k+1} = D\cdot 2^{-(k+1)}$, so
\begin{align*}
     \sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},D\,2^{-(k+1)})}
      = 2 & \sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},D\,2^{-k})}\\
     =  & \frac{4}{D}\sum_{k=0}^\infty (\epsilon_{k}-\epsilon_{k+1})\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon_{k})}\\
     = & \frac{4}{D}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\int_{\epsilon_{k+1}}^{\epsilon_{k}} \sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon_{k})} \mathrm{d}\epsilon\\
     \leq & \frac{4}{D}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\int_{\epsilon_{k+1}}^{\epsilon_{k}} \sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon)} \mathrm{d}\epsilon\\
     = & \frac{4}{D}\int_{0}^{D}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon)} \mathrm{d}\epsilon
\end{align*}
where the inequality holds because the right-endpoint Riemann sum of a decreasing function underestimates the integral. Applying this bound to each of the four spaces and invoking the covering-number lemmas:

\noindent\textbf{(i) $\mathcal{F}_f$ and $\mathcal{F}_p$.}
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:covering_number_Linfty},
\[
\frac{2}{D_f}\int_0^{D_f}\!\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;\leq\; \frac{2}{D_f}\sqrt{\frac{V(d_\mathcal{D}+2)}{2}}
\int_0^{D_f}\!\sqrt{\log\frac{C'D_f}{\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon.
\]
Using the substitution $\epsilon = D_f e^{-t}$ from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:covering_number_Linfty}:
\[
\frac{1}{D_f}\int_0^{D_f}\!\sqrt{\log\frac{C'D_f}{\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
= \int_0^\infty\!\sqrt{\log C' + t}\;e^{-t}\,\mathrm{d}t
\;\leq\; \sqrt{\log C'} + \Gamma\!\left(\tfrac{3}{2}\right) < \infty.
\]
Hence $\dfrac{2}{D_f}\int_0^{D_f}\!\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon \leq C_{f} < \infty$, a universal constant independent of $n$, $\lambda_n$, and $\gamma_n$. The same bound holds for $\mathcal{F}_p$ with constant $C_p'<\infty$.

\noindent\textbf{(ii) $\Psi$.}
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:covering_number_psi} and Step~4 of its proof,
\[
\frac{2}{D_\psi}\int_0^{D_\psi}\!\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Psi,\|\cdot\|_\Psi,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;\leq\; \frac{2}{D_\psi}\sqrt{d_\Psi}\int_0^{D_\psi}\!\sqrt{\log\frac{D_\psi}{\epsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;=\; \sqrt{d_\Psi\,\pi} \;=:\; C_\psi < \infty,
\]
where $d_\Psi = \dim\Psi$ and we used the substitution $\epsilon = D_\psi e^{-t}$.

\noindent\textbf{(iii) $B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2})$.}
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:covering_number_rkhs}, for any $p\in(0,1)$, with $D_\theta = 2\lambda_n^{-1/2}$:
\begin{align*}
\frac{2}{D_\theta}\int_0^{D_\theta}\!\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
&\;\leq\; \frac{2\sqrt{C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}}{D_\theta}
\int_0^{D_\theta}\!\epsilon^{-p}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon\\
&\;=\; \frac{2\sqrt{C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}}{D_\theta}\cdot\frac{D_\theta^{1-p}}{1-p}\\
&\;=\; \frac{2\sqrt{C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}}{1-p}\cdot D_\theta^{-p}.
\end{align*}
Substituting $D_\theta^{-p} = (2\lambda_n^{-1/2})^{-p} = 2^{-p}\lambda_n^{p/2}$:
\[
\frac{2}{D_\theta}\int_0^{D_\theta}\!\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(B_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_n}}(\lambda_n^{-1/2}),\|\cdot\|_\infty,\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
\;\leq\; \frac{2^{1-p}\sqrt{C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}}{1-p}.
\]
The factors $\lambda_n^{-p/2}$ (from the covering-number bound) and $\lambda_n^{p/2}$ (from $D_\theta^{-p}$) cancel exactly, leaving no dependence on $\lambda_n$.

Combining (i)--(iii):
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dudley_2k}
\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}}
\;\leq\; C_{f} + C_{f}' + C_\psi + \frac{2^{1-p}\sqrt{C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}}{1-p}
\;\leq\; C\,\gamma_n^{-d/2},
\end{equation}
where $C<\infty$ absorbs all constants from parts~(i)--(ii), and the last step uses $\gamma_n \leq 1$.

\noindent\textbf{Step 3b: Bounding $\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}}$.}
The same square-root subadditivity splits this into four pieces. Observing that $2^{-k/2} = \sqrt{2}\,(\epsilon_{k+1}/D)^{1/2}$ and $\epsilon_k - \epsilon_{k+1} = \epsilon_{k+1}$, the sum is a right-endpoint Riemann sum for the weighted integrand $\epsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon)}$:
\[
\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},D\,2^{-(k+1)})}
\;=\; \frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{D}}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\epsilon_{k+1}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon_{k+1})}\,(\epsilon_k-\epsilon_{k+1})
\;\leq\; \frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{D}}\int_0^D\!\epsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon.
\]
For the fixed spaces $\mathcal{F}_f$, $\mathcal{F}_p$, and $\Psi$, whose covering numbers are at most logarithmic, a direct bound using Lemma~\ref{lemma:covering_number_Linfty} gives:
\[
\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_f,D_f2^{-(k+1)})}
\;\leq\; \sqrt{\frac{V(d_\mathcal{D}+2)}{2}}\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{(k+1)\log 2 + \log C'}
\;<\;\infty,
\]
since $\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{k} < \infty$ (the geometric factor dominates). For the RKHS ball, the weighted integral bound with Lemma~\ref{lemma:covering_number_rkhs} gives, for $p\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2})$:
\begin{align*}
