\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{D_\theta}}\int_0^{D_\theta}\!\epsilon^{-1/2}\sqrt{C_p\gamma_n^{-d}\lambda_n^{-p}}\,\epsilon^{-p}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon
&\;=\; \frac{\sqrt{2C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}}{\sqrt{D_\theta}}\cdot\frac{D_\theta^{1/2-p}}{1/2-p}
\;=\; \frac{2^{1/2-p}\sqrt{C_p}\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}}{1/2-p},
\end{align*}
where $\lambda_n^{-p/2}\cdot D_\theta^{-p} = \lambda_n^{-p/2}\cdot(2\lambda_n^{-1/2})^{-p} = 2^{-p}\lambda_n^{-p/2+p/2}=2^{-p}$ cancels $\lambda_n$ exactly. The integral converges for $p\in(0,\frac{1}{2})$ (so that $1/2-p > 0$). Combining:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dudley_2k_half}
\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}} \;\leq\; C\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}, \qquad p\in\left(0,\tfrac{1}{2}\right).
\end{equation}

\noindent\textbf{Final bound for Term~3.}
Substituting \eqref{eq:dudley_2k} and \eqref{eq:dudley_2k_half} into Term~3 (choosing $p\in(0,\frac{1}{2})$ so that both bounds hold):
\begin{align*}
C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{2\log N_{k+1}}
&\;=\; C\sqrt{2}\left(\sigma_0^{(1)}\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}}
+\sigma_0^{(2)}\sum_{k=0}^\infty 2^{-k/2}\sqrt{\log N_{k+1}}\right)\\
&\;\leq\; C\,\gamma_n^{-d/2}\!\left(\sigma_0^{(1)}+\sigma_0^{(2)}\right).
\end{align*}

\noindent \textbf{Bounding Term 2:} The chaining decomposition of $\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)$ is:
\begin{align*}
   \bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi) &= \underbrace{\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))}_{\text{base term}}\\
   & +\sum_{k=0}^\infty\underbrace{\left\{\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k+1}(p),\pi_{k+1}(\psi)) - \bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_{k}(f),\pi_{k}(p),\pi_{k}(\psi))\right\}}_{\text{$k$-th increment: }\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)}. 
\end{align*}
By the sub-Gaussianity of the empirical risk in equation \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_L}, and the union bound,
\begin{align*}
&\pr\left(\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))\right|\geq t \right)\\
    \leq & |\mathcal{C}_\theta| \pr\left(\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))\right|\geq t \right)\\
    \leq & 2|\mathcal{C}_\theta|\exp\left(-\frac{Ct^2}{B_L(n)^2}\right).
\end{align*}
Thus, with probability no less than $1-2e^{-\tau}$, the base term is bounded by
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))\right|\leq CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau +\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)}
\end{align*}
Similar to 
By the sub-Gaussianity w.r.t. $f,p$ and $\psi$ in Equation \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_f}, \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_p}, \eqref{eq:sub_gaussian_psi}, the $k$-th increment satisfies:
$$\|\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)\|_{\psi_2}\leq \sigma_k = \sigma_02^{-k}.$$
By the union bound, for any $\tau_k>0$, 
\begin{align*}
    \pr\left(\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta,\psi,f,p}|\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)|\geq t\right)
    \leq N_{k+1}^2|\mathcal{C}_\theta| \pr\left(|\Delta_k|\geq t\right)
    \leq 2N_{k+1}^2|\mathcal{C}_\theta| \exp\left(-\frac{Ct^2}{\sigma_k^2}\right)
\end{align*}
Thus, for any $\tau_k>0$, with probability no less than $1-2e^{-\tau_k}$, 
$$\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta,\psi,f,p}|\Delta_k(\theta,\psi,f,p)|\leq C\sigma_k\sqrt{\tau_k +2\log(N_k)+\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)}.$$
Combine the high probability bound for base term and $k$-th incremen and let $\tau_k = \tau+k$, we have that with probability no less than $1-2e^{-\tau}(1+\sum_{k=1}^\infty e^{-k})\geq 1- 6e^{-\tau}$, 
\begin{align*}
    & \sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta,\psi,f,p}|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)|\\
    \leq & \sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_0(f),\pi_0(p),\pi_0(\psi))\right|\\
    & +\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}_\theta,\psi,f,p}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k+1}(p),\pi_{k+1}(\psi)) - \bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;\pi_{k}(f),\pi_{k}(p),\pi_{k}(\psi))\right|\\
    \leq & CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau +\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)} + C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{\tau + k +2\log(N_k)+\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)}\\
    \leq & CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau +\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)}+C\sqrt{\tau}\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k + C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{k} + C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{2\log(N_k)} + C\sum_{k=0}^\infty\sigma_k\sqrt{2\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)}\\
    \leq & CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau +\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)} + \sigma_0(1+\sqrt{\tau}+\sqrt{\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)})
\end{align*}

\noindent\textbf{Finalizing} Combining the Upper Bound of Term 1 and Term 2, with probability no less than $1-12e^{-\tau}$, we have 
\begin{align*}
&\sup_{\theta,\psi,f,p}|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)|\\
    =&\sup_{\theta,\psi,f,p}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)-\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi(\theta);f,p,\psi)+\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi(\theta);f,p,\psi)\right|\\
    \leq & \underbrace{\sup_{|\theta-\theta^\prime|_\infty\leq \epsilon_\theta,\psi,f,p}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)-\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\pi(\theta);f,p,\psi)\right|}_{\text{\textbf{Term 1}}}+\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in \mathcal{C}_\theta,\psi,f,p}\left|\bar{R}_n^{(\delta_n)}(\theta;f,p,\psi)\right|}_{\text{\textbf{Term 2}}}\\
    \leq & \delta_n^{-1}\epsilon_\theta+CB_L(n)\sqrt{\tau +\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)} + \sigma_0(1+\sqrt{\tau}+\sqrt{\log(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)}) \\
    % \leq & B_\psi(n)(1+\sqrt{\tau}) + B_\psi(n) C_p\gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}\epsilon_\theta^{-p} + C\sqrt{\tau}(B^1_\theta(n) \epsilon_\theta + B^2_\theta(n) \epsilon_\theta^{1/2})
    \leq & B_\psi(n)(1+\sqrt{\tau}) + B_\psi(n) C_p\gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}\epsilon_\theta^{-p} + \delta_n^{-1}\epsilon_\theta
\end{align*}
The last inequality follows from (i) $B_\psi(n)$ dominates $B_p(n)$, $B_L(n)$, $B_f(n)$, i.e., $\sigma_0 \asymp B_\psi(n)$; (ii) According to Lemma \ref{thm:equivalence_covering_entropy}  and Lemma \ref{thm:entropy_gaussian}, $$\ln(|\mathcal{C}_\theta|)\leq C_p\gamma_n^{-d}\lambda_n^{-p}\epsilon_\theta^{-2p}\text{ for any }p\in(0,1).$$ 
Define $K_n := B_\psi(n) C_p \gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}$. We choose $\epsilon_\theta \asymp (K_n\delta_n)^{1/(p+1)} $ to balance the rate of $\delta_n^{-1}\epsilon_\theta$ and $K_n\epsilon_\theta^{-p}$. Then, the final rate is $\delta_n^{-p/(p+1)}K_n^{1/(p+1)}$.

If we further combine it with the plug-in error in Lemma \ref{lemma:risk_function_plug_in_error} and the approximation error in Lemma \ref{lemma:svm_approximation_error}. We have the excess risk of SVM as:
\begin{align*}
    B_\psi(n)(1+\sqrt{\tau}) + \delta_n^{-p/(p+1)}K_n^{1/(p+1)} +\delta_n +r_{\mu,n}r_{p,n} + \lambda_n \gamma_n^{-d} + \gamma_n^\beta.
\end{align*}



% Define $K_n = B_\psi(n) C_p \gamma_n^{-d/2}\lambda_n^{-p/2}$. If we choose  $\epsilon_\theta$ to balance $B^1_\theta(n) \epsilon_\theta$ and $K_n\epsilon_\theta^{-p}$, we have $\epsilon_{\theta, 1} \asymp \left( K_n/B^1_\theta(n) \right)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}$. If we choose $\epsilon_\theta$ to balance $B^2_\theta(n) \epsilon_\theta^{1/2}$ and $K_n\epsilon_\theta^{-p}$, we have

%     B^2_\theta(n) = C \log(n) n^{-1/2} \|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}} \gamma_n^{-1} \lambda_n^{-1/4}\\
%     B_\psi(n)  = C\left(\delta_n^{-1}+\|\Sigma_n\|_{\text{op}}(1+\gamma_n^{-1})\right)\gamma_n^{-1}\lambda_n^{-1/2}\log(n)^{3/2}\,n^{-1/2}
